# online-search

> **⚠️ Disclaimer:** All the code in this repository was generated by Claude Code and has not been verified. Use it at your own risk.

Small standalone scripts that pull live data from public web APIs, plus two small local web apps built on top of them. Each script is self-contained — no local package, just a `.py` file; where scripts overlap they import small shared helper modules from the same directory (`yahoo_finance.py`, `daily_prices.py`, `weather.py`, `hn.py`, `indicators.py`, `valuation.py`, `cli_utils.py`) instead of duplicating logic.

> **Windows: make sure `python3` is a real Python.** Every example here, and every Claude Code agent in `.claude/agents/`, runs scripts as `python3 …`. The python.org installer for Windows doesn't create a `python3.exe`, so `python3` usually resolves to the Microsoft Store placeholder in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WindowsApps\`. That prints "Python was not found; run without arguments to install from the Microsoft Store…" and fails (exit code 9009 in PowerShell / cmd, 49 from Git Bash), so an agent's first command fails. Fix it in the environment, not the repo (on macOS and Linux, `python` may be missing or Python 2):
>
> - **With a virtualenv** (what this repo is used with): copy its `Scripts\python.exe` to `Scripts\python3.exe`. While that virtualenv is first on `PATH`, `python3` runs the same interpreter with the same packages. Recreating the virtualenv removes the copy, so repeat this after a rebuild.
> - **Optionally**, turn off the placeholder: Settings → Apps → Advanced app settings → App execution aliases → "python3.exe" off.
>
> Check with `python3 --version` in both PowerShell and Git Bash.

**[Weather](#weather)**
- [`weather.py`](#weatherpy) — current weather (temperature, condition, wind) for a named location, anywhere in the world
- [`.claude/agents/weather.md`](#claude-code-agent-weather) — Claude Code subagent that calls `weather.py` and reports the current weather for a named location
- [`weather_forecast.py`](#weather_forecastpy) — multi-day (1-16 day) weather forecast for a named location, with the daily UV index and sunrise / sunset / daylight hours, sharing `weather.py`'s geocoding
- [`.claude/agents/weather-forecast.md`](#claude-code-agent-weather-forecast) — Claude Code subagent that calls `weather_forecast.py` and reports the upcoming multi-day forecast, UV index or sunrise / sunset for a named location
- [`weather_history.py`](#weather_historypy) — past weather for a named location on a date or over a range (back to 1940), with a range summary, from Open-Meteo's archive, sharing `weather.py`'s geocoding
- [`.claude/agents/weather-history.md`](#claude-code-agent-weather-history) — Claude Code subagent that calls `weather_history.py` and reports the weather on a past date or period at a named location
- [`air_quality.py`](#air_qualitypy) — current air quality (US AQI, European AQI, pollutant levels, and pollen counts in Europe) for a named location, or with `--forecast` each coming day's worst AQI and pollen (about 4–5 days ahead), sharing `weather.py`'s geocoding
- [`.claude/agents/air-quality.md`](#claude-code-agent-air-quality) — Claude Code subagent that calls `air_quality.py` and reports the current air quality / AQI for a named location
- [`trip_weather.py`](#trip_weatherpy) — weather for a trip (a place and up to 31 days, up to a year ahead): the forecast where there is one, typical weather from the same dates in past years beyond it, UV, sun times, and air quality and pollen where forecast, merged by date from the other weather scripts
- [`.claude/agents/trip-weather.md`](#claude-code-agent-trip-weather) — Claude Code subagent that calls `trip_weather.py` and reports the weather for a trip, keeping forecast and typical weather clearly apart

**[Market & Stock Data](#market--stock-data)**
- [`market_top_volume.py`](#market_top_volumepy) — top movers (volume, gainers, or losers) on any of ~20 world markets
- [`.claude/agents/market-top-volume.md`](#claude-code-agent-market-top-volume) — Claude Code subagent that calls `market_top_volume.py` and reports the top movers (volume / gainers / losers) on a named market
- [`stock_price_history.py`](#stock_price_historypy) — past daily open / high / low / close prices for a single stock
- [`.claude/agents/stock-price-history.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-price-history) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_price_history.py` and reports past open / high / low / close prices of a stock, index, currency pair, crypto coin or commodity future
- [`.claude/agents/stock-trend.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-trend) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_price_history.py` + `indicators.py` and reports whether a named stock, index, currency pair, crypto coin or commodity future is trending up or down
- [`stock_candlestick_chart.py`](#stock_candlestick_chartpy) — candlestick (OHLC) price chart for a single stock, with moving-average overlays and cross-flip arrows, rendered to a PNG with matplotlib
- [`.claude/agents/stock-candlestick-chart.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-candlestick-chart) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_candlestick_chart.py` and produces a candlestick chart for a named stock, index, currency pair, crypto coin or commodity future
- [`stock_rebased_chart.py`](#stock_rebased_chartpy) — rebases a series of stocks' closing prices to 100 as of a common date and plots them together, rendered to a PNG with matplotlib
- [`.claude/agents/stock-rebased-chart.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-rebased-chart) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_rebased_chart.py` and produces a rebased (indexed-to-100) comparison chart for several named stocks, indices, currency pairs, crypto coins or commodity futures
- [`stock_risk.py`](#stock_riskpy) — how risky one or more instruments' prices have been: annualized volatility, the worst peak-to-trough drawdown and its recovery, distance from the high, and the correlation of each pair, via `indicators.py`
- [`.claude/agents/stock-risk.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-risk) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_risk.py` and reports how volatile named stocks, indices, currency pairs, crypto coins or commodity futures have been, their worst falls, and how closely they moved together
- [`yahoo_finance.py`](#yahoo_financepy) — shared helper module for the price scripts' window flags and chart-result parsing (their data now comes from `price_source.py`), Yahoo's chart-endpoint fetch (still used by `app/stock_information/` for its quote) and the cookie + crumb `quoteSummary` fetch, used by the four price scripts, `market_top_volume.py`, `stock_intrinsic_value.py`, `stock_earnings_calendar.py`, `stock_analyst_targets.py`, `stock_holders.py` and `app/stock_information/`
- [`price_source.py`](#price_sourcepy) — shared price-history source for the four price scripts: routes a Yahoo-notation ticker to EODHD (Alpha Vantage for India) and returns the bars in the shape they already parse; Singapore, Tokyo and futures are refused for now
- [`eodhd_price_history.py`](#eodhd_price_historypy) — past daily open / high / low / close prices of a stock, index, currency pair or crypto coin from EODHD's end-of-day API (requires a free `EODHD_API_KEY`); the main provider behind `price_source.py`
- [`alphavantage_price_history.py`](#alphavantage_price_historypy) — past daily open / high / low / close prices of a stock from Alpha Vantage's `TIME_SERIES_DAILY` API (requires a free `ALPHAVANTAGE_API_KEY`; free keys get the latest 100 sessions)
- [`daily_prices.py`](#daily_pricespy) — shared helper module for the two keyed price scripts: the `--start`/`--end`/`--last` window, the price row and the text table
- [`app/stock_information/`](#appstock_information) — small local web app: enter a ticker and see the company (name, sector, industry, market cap, P/E, dividend yield), price (current, session move, previous close, day and 52-week range bars), fundamental indicators (profitability, growth, valuation multiples, financial health, cash flow, shareholder returns) and intrinsic-value estimates (undervalued / fair value / overvalued counts plus a table), via `yahoo_finance.py`, `stock_fundamental.py` and `stock_intrinsic_value.py`

**[Valuation & Fundamental Analysis](#valuation--fundamental-analysis)**
- [`indicators.py`](#indicatorspy) — dependency-free technical indicators and risk metrics over a price series: `moving_average`, `price_vs_moving_average`, `moving_average_cross`, `moving_average_cross_flip`, `rebase`, `trend`, `returns`, `volatility`, `max_drawdown`, `correlation`
- [`valuation.py`](#valuationpy) — dependency-free intrinsic-value estimators: `gordon_growth_value`, `discounted_cash_flow`, `multiple_value` / `ev_multiple_value`, `equity_from_enterprise`, `implied_growth_rate` (reverse DCF)
- [`stock_intrinsic_value.py`](#stock_intrinsic_valuepy) — pulls fundamentals from Yahoo Finance and runs every `valuation.py` method (DCF, reverse DCF, dividend discount, P/E, Graham, EV/EBITDA, P/S) against the current price
- [`.claude/agents/stock-intrinsic-value.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-intrinsic-value) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_intrinsic_value.py` and reports a named stock's DCF / reverse-DCF / multiples fair value versus its price
- [`stock_fundamental.py`](#stock_fundamentalpy) — pulls a stock's fundamental indicators from Yahoo Finance: margins, ROE/ROA, growth, P/E, PEG, EV/EBITDA, P/B, P/S, FCF yield, debt and liquidity ratios, cash flow, dividend yield and payout ratio (also used by `app/stock_information/`)
- [`.claude/agents/stock-fundamental.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-fundamental) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_fundamental.py` and reports and interprets a named stock's fundamental indicators
- [`stock_earnings_calendar.py`](#stock_earnings_calendarpy) — pulls a stock's next earnings date with the EPS and revenue estimates, its ex-dividend and payment dates, and its last four quarters of EPS against estimates from Yahoo Finance
- [`.claude/agents/stock-earnings-calendar.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-earnings-calendar) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_earnings_calendar.py` and answers when a named stock reports, what's expected, whether it beat, and its dividend dates
- [`stock_analyst_targets.py`](#stock_analyst_targetspy) — pulls a stock's analyst price targets (low / median / mean / high, with upside), consensus rating, four-month buy / hold / sell trend and recent firm-level upgrades / downgrades from Yahoo Finance
- [`.claude/agents/stock-analyst-targets.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-analyst-targets) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_analyst_targets.py` and summarizes what analysts think of a named stock
- [`stock_holders.py`](#stock_holderspy) — pulls a stock's ownership breakdown (insiders / institutions), recent insider transactions sorted into sales, purchases, awards, gifts and buybacks, and its top fund and institutional holders from Yahoo Finance
- [`.claude/agents/stock-holders.md`](#claude-code-agent-stock-holders) — Claude Code subagent that calls `stock_holders.py` and reports who owns a named stock and what its insiders have been doing
- [`docs/`](docs/) — plain-English guides to the [indicators](docs/indicators.md) (`indicators.py`) and the [valuation methods](docs/valuations.md) (`valuation.py`), for readers who want the concepts without the API detail; plus a feature spec (`features.md`) for every [script](docs/features/script/) and [agent](docs/features/agent/)

- [`stock_tech_buzz_agent.py`](#stock_tech_buzz_agentpy) — agent combining two of the above: top-volume stocks that are being talked about on Hacker News

- [`web_search_duckduckgo.py`](#web_search_duckduckgopy) — top web search results for a query, by scraping DuckDuckGo via the `ddgs` package (no API key, but unofficial and rate-limit-prone)
- [`web_search_serpapi.py`](#web_search_serpapipy) — top web search results for a query, via the SerpApi Google Search API (requires a free `SERPAPI_API_KEY`)
- [`web_search_serper.py`](#web_search_serperpy) — top web search results for a query, via the Serper Google Search API (requires a free `SERPER_API_KEY`)
- [`web_search_tavily.py`](#web_search_tavilypy) — top web search results for a query, via the Tavily Search API — its own crawl/index built for LLM/agent use (requires a free `TAVILY_API_KEY`)
- [`web_search_exa.py`](#web_search_exapy) — top web search results for a query, via the Exa neural/semantic Search API (requires a free `EXA_API_KEY`)
- [`web_search_perplexity.py`](#web_search_perplexitypy) — a synthesized, cited answer to a query, via the Perplexity Sonar API (requires a paid `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY`; a different output shape from the other five — see its section below)
- [`.claude/agents/web-search.md`](#claude-code-agent-web-search) — Claude Code subagent that tries, in order, `web_search_brave.py`, `web_search_serper.py`, `web_search_tavily.py`, `web_search_serpapi.py`, `web_search_exa.py`, then `web_search_duckduckgo.py`, and reports the first one that succeeds (`web_search_perplexity.py` is a standalone tool, not part of this fallback chain)
- [`url_availability.py`](#url_availabilitypy) — checks whether a web page at a given URL is reachable, via an HTTP GET request and status-code classification, optionally finding a dead page's last Wayback Machine copy (no API key)
- [`.claude/agents/url-availability.md`](#claude-code-agent-url-availability) — Claude Code subagent that calls `url_availability.py` and reports whether a named URL is up, down, or returning an error, with a dead page's Wayback Machine copy on request
- [`url_broken_links.py`](#url_broken_linkspy) — checks every link on a web page (via `url_links.py` and `url_availability.py`) and reports the broken, unreachable and blocked ones, politely: a link cap, a per-host delay, robots.txt honoured for each linked site, optional Wayback copies (no API key)
- [`.claude/agents/url-broken-links.md`](#claude-code-agent-url-broken-links) — Claude Code subagent that calls `url_broken_links.py` and reports which links on a named page are broken, with archived copies on request
- [`url_content.py`](#url_contentpy) — retrieves the raw content (HTML/text/JSON/etc.) of a web page at a given URL, via an HTTP GET request, with an optional `--text` mode (BeautifulSoup) to extract just the human-readable text (no API key), and `--respect-robots` to honour the site's robots.txt and AI opt-out first
- [`.claude/agents/url-content.md`](#claude-code-agent-url-content) — Claude Code subagent that calls `url_content.py` and reports or summarizes the content at a named URL
- [`url_links.py`](#url_linkspy) — retrieves a web page at a given URL and extracts every hyperlink (`<a href>`) in its HTML content, resolved to absolute URLs, via BeautifulSoup (no API key)
- [`.claude/agents/url-links.md`](#claude-code-agent-url-links) — Claude Code subagent that calls `url_links.py` and reports the links found on a named URL
- [`url_feed.py`](#url_feedpy) — the latest items from a site's RSS or Atom feed, given the feed or a page that links to it, parsed safely with `defusedxml` and listed newest first, with `--respect-robots` / `--warn-robots` (no API key)
- [`.claude/agents/url-feed.md`](#claude-code-agent-url-feed) — Claude Code subagent that calls `url_feed.py` and lists a site's latest posts or releases
- [`url_metadata.py`](#url_metadatapy) — retrieves a web page at a given URL and extracts its title, meta description, canonical URL, and OpenGraph / Twitter-card tags, via BeautifulSoup (no API key)
- [`.claude/agents/url-metadata.md`](#claude-code-agent-url-metadata) — Claude Code subagent that calls `url_metadata.py` and reports the title, description, canonical URL or link-preview tags of one or more URLs
- [`.claude/agents/url-summary.md`](#claude-code-agent-url-summary) — Claude Code subagent that calls `url_content.py` once per given URL and reports a summary of each page's content

**[Development](#development)**
- [`tests/`](tests/) — offline `pytest` suite for every script, helper module and web app (see [`tests/README.md`](tests/README.md))
- [`cli_utils.py`](#cli_utilspy) — small shared CLI helpers (`use_utf8_output()` for UTF-8 stdout/stderr on every platform, `die()` uniform error-exit, `validate_url_scheme()` and `response_text()` (charset-aware page decoding) for the `url_*.py` scripts, `positive_int` / `non_negative_int` / `bounded_int` argparse types, `.env` loading and `require_env()` for API keys, result-list formatting/printing) imported by the command-line scripts

---

# Weather

---

## weather.py

Prints the current weather — condition, temperature, and wind — for a named location anywhere in the world, using [Open-Meteo](https://open-meteo.com/)'s public geocoding and forecast APIs. No API key or authentication required.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `cli_utils.py` module (no install — run from the repo root so it imports)

```bash
pip install requests
```

### Usage

```bash
python3 weather.py LOCATION [--unit {celsius,fahrenheit}] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `LOCATION` (positional) | — | Free-text place name, e.g. `"London"`, `"New York"`, `"Tokyo"` |
| `--unit {celsius,fahrenheit}` | `celsius` | Temperature unit |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report — for calling this script as a tool from an agent or another program |

#### Example output

```
Looking up the weather for 'Tokyo'...

Weather for Tokyo, Japan
  Condition: Light drizzle
  Temperature: 26.5°C
  Wind: 2.5 km/h, direction 360°
  As of: 2026-09-08T07:45

Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.
```

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 weather.py "New York" --json
```

```json
{
  "location": "New York",
  "admin1": "New York",
  "country": "United States",
  "latitude": 40.71427,
  "longitude": -74.00597,
  "temperature": 16.6,
  "unit": "°C",
  "windspeed": 5.0,
  "winddirection": 291,
  "weather_code": 0,
  "condition": "Clear sky",
  "time": "2026-09-08T07:45",
  "attribution": "Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0."
}
```

With `--json`, the leading progress line is suppressed and the result prints as a single JSON object on stdout. On failure, an exit code of `1` is returned and a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) is printed to stderr instead of plain text.

### How it works

1. **Geocode** — Calls Open-Meteo's geocoding endpoint (`geocoding-api.open-meteo.com/v1/search`) with the free-text `LOCATION` and takes its top match (name, admin region, country, latitude/longitude).
2. **Fetch current weather** — Calls the forecast endpoint (`api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast`) with `current_weather=true` at that location's coordinates and the requested `--unit`.
3. **Decode and display** — Maps the numeric WMO `weathercode` to a short description (`WEATHER_CODES`) and prints location, condition, temperature, wind speed/direction, and the observation time (or the equivalent JSON object with `--json`).

### Configuration

These are set as constants near the top of the file — edit them directly to change behavior:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` | 10 | Per-request timeout in seconds |
| `SECONDS_PER_DAY` | 86400 | Daylight at or above this is midnight sun in `sun_fields()` |
| `WEATHER_CODES` | dict of ~27 entries | Maps each WMO `weathercode` to a short human-readable description |

### API reference

- `fetch_json(url, params)` — GETs an Open-Meteo endpoint and returns the JSON body. On an error status it raises `requests.HTTPError`; when Open-Meteo explains the rejection (`{"error": true, "reason": "..."}`), the message is `"<status>: <reason>"` rather than a bare status line.
- `geocode(location)` — Looks up a free-text location name and returns its top-matching place dict (name, admin1, country, latitude, longitude), or `None` if nothing matches.
- `fetch_current_weather(latitude, longitude, unit="celsius")` — Fetches the `current_weather` object for a coordinate pair.
- `place_fields(place)` — Returns the `{location, admin1, country, latitude, longitude}` fields every record built from a `geocode()` match starts with. Shared with `weather_forecast.py`'s `get_forecast()`, `weather_history.py`'s `get_history()` and `air_quality.py`'s `get_air_quality()`.
- `weather_description(code)` — Maps a WMO weather code to a short description, or `"Unknown (code N)"` for one not in `WEATHER_CODES`.
- `daily_value(daily, key, i)` — Day `i` of an Open-Meteo `daily` block's `key` series, or `None` when the series or day is missing.
- `local_daily(data)` — The `daily` block of an Open-Meteo response with sunrise and sunset moved to each date's own UTC offset. Open-Meteo gives every date the offset the place has today, so across a daylight-saving change its times are an hour out (in October 2026 it gave Orlando's 2025-12-20 sunset as 18:33 EDT instead of 17:33 EST, and its forecast kept 18:39 after the 1 November change). The response names the zone and the offset it used, so each time is converted through UTC into that zone with `zoneinfo` (Windows needs the `tzdata` package); without a zone database the block is returned unchanged. Used by `weather_forecast.fetch_daily_forecast()` and `weather_history.fetch_daily_history()`, found by the `trip-weather` test runs on 2026-10-21.
- `sun_fields(daily, i)` — `{sunrise, sunset, daylight_hours, polar}` for day `i` of a `daily` block requested with `sunrise`, `sunset` and `daylight_duration`: local `"HH:MM"` times (the scripts call the API with `timezone=auto`) and daylight in hours. With 0 h of daylight (`polar: "night"`) or 24 h (`polar: "day"`), Open-Meteo's midnight placeholders become `None`. Shared by `weather_forecast.py` and `weather_history.py`.
- `format_sun(day)` — `(sunrise, sunset)` display strings for a record carrying `sun_fields()`: the times, `("polar night", "")`, `("midnight sun", "")`, or `("-", "-")`.
- `format_daylight(hours)` — Daylight hours as `"12h05m"`, or `"-"` when missing.
- `get_weather(location, unit="celsius")` — Orchestrates geocoding and the weather fetch; returns the flattened `{location, admin1, country, latitude, longitude, temperature, unit, windspeed, winddirection, weather_code, condition, time}` record, or `None` if the location can't be found.
- `format_place(row)` — Builds a `"City, Region, Country"` label from a geocoded record (the region is dropped when it just repeats the city, the country appended when present). Shared with `weather_forecast.py`'s `format_forecast()` and `weather_history.py`'s `format_history()`.
- `format_weather(row)` — Renders a `get_weather()` record as a multi-line human-readable string (its place line comes from `format_place()`).
- `parse_args(argv=None)` — Parses the positional `LOCATION` plus `--unit` and `--json`.
- `main(argv=None)` — Entry point; geocodes, fetches, handles top-level network/not-found errors, and prints the result (human-readable or JSON, depending on `--json`).

### Error handling

- If either API request fails (network error, timeout, non-2xx response), the script prints an error and exits with status code 1 — plain text on stderr normally, or a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) on stderr when `--json` is passed.
- If the location can't be geocoded (no match), the script prints `Error: No location found matching '<LOCATION>'.` (or `{"error": "No location found matching '<LOCATION>'."}` with `--json`) to stderr and exits with status code 1.

### Notes / limitations

- **Attribution is required.** Open-Meteo's data is licensed [CC BY 4.0](https://open-meteo.com/en/terms), so the report always ends with `Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.` and the JSON carries the same text as `attribution` (the constant `weather.ATTRIBUTION`, shared by all four Open-Meteo scripts). Keep it when passing results on. Free use is for non-commercial purposes, under 10,000 calls a day.
- Geocoding always takes Open-Meteo's single top match for the query string — an ambiguous name (e.g. a city that exists in several countries) may resolve to the wrong place; disambiguate by adding a country or region to the query (e.g. `"Springfield, Illinois"`).
- Reports only the current conditions at a single instant — no forecast, hourly, or historical data (see [`weather_forecast.py`](#weather_forecastpy) and [`weather_history.py`](#weather_historypy) for those).
- `weathercode` is Open-Meteo's own WMO-based classification; `WEATHER_CODES` covers the documented codes but treats any other value as `"Unknown (code N)"` rather than guessing.
- Results are a live snapshot; conditions will differ between runs.

---

## Claude Code agent: `weather`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/weather.md`. It calls no API itself — it shells out to [`weather.py`](#weatherpy) via the `Bash` tool and reports the result conversationally.

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer questions like "what's the weather in Tokyo?", "is it raining in London?", "how hot is it in Singapore right now?", or "weather for Long Island" by running the script and summarizing its JSON output, instead of guessing or using stale training data.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for current-conditions/temperature/weather questions about a named place, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "weather"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Passes the user's location text through to the tool mostly as-is, only adding a country/state qualifier itself when the name looks ambiguous and the user gave enough context to disambiguate.
2. Runs `python3 weather.py "LOCATION" [--unit celsius|fahrenheit] --json` from the repo root, defaulting to `celsius` unless the user asks for Fahrenheit or the conversation implies it.
3. Parses the JSON object (`location`, `admin1`, `country`, `temperature`, `unit`, `windspeed`, `winddirection`, `condition`, `time`) and leads with the condition and temperature, adding wind or the resolved place name only when useful. If the command fails, it surfaces the `{"error": "..."}` from stderr — a "no location found" result is reported as-is rather than retried, while a network-looking failure is retried at most once.

### Configuration

The agent's frontmatter restricts it to the `Bash` tool only, since running the script and reading its stdout is all it needs.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`weather.py`](#weatherpy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Ends its report with a credit line for Open-Meteo.com, whose data is licensed CC BY 4.0 and requires attribution.
- Requires `weather.py` (and its `requests` dependency) to be present and runnable from the repo root.
- Reports current conditions only — there is no forecast, hourly, or historical data to fall back on, so it says so rather than fabricating one.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`weather.py`](#weatherpy) itself (geocoding takes only the top match for an ambiguous name, live-snapshot results).

---

## weather_forecast.py

Prints the multi-day weather forecast — condition, high/low temperature, rain chance, and max wind, plus the day's peak UV index (with its WHO category) and sunrise, sunset and daylight hours — for a named location, using [Open-Meteo](https://open-meteo.com/)'s public geocoding and forecast APIs. No API key or authentication required.

Requires `weather.py` to be present in the same directory — it imports `geocode`, `fetch_json`, `weather_description`, `format_place`, `place_fields`, the daily-series helpers `daily_value`, `sun_fields`, `format_sun` and `format_daylight`, and `FORECAST_URL` directly from it rather than shelling out or duplicating that logic (the same sharing pattern [`stock_tech_buzz_agent.py`](#stock_tech_buzz_agentpy) uses for its two underlying scripts). It also imports `die` from [`cli_utils.py`](#cli_utilspy).

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `weather.py` and `cli_utils.py` modules (no install — run from the repo root so they import)

```bash
pip install requests
```

### Usage

```bash
python3 weather_forecast.py LOCATION [--days N] [--unit {celsius,fahrenheit}] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `LOCATION` (positional) | — | Free-text place name, e.g. `"London"`, `"New York"`, `"Tokyo"` |
| `--days N` | 5 | Number of forecast days, starting today. Must be 1-16 (Open-Meteo's own daily-forecast limit); other values are rejected by argument parsing |
| `--unit {celsius,fahrenheit}` | `celsius` | Temperature unit |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report — for calling this script as a tool from an agent or another program |

#### Example output

```
Looking up the 3-day forecast for 'Tokyo'...

3-day forecast for Tokyo, Japan

Date        Condition                    High °C    Low °C  Precip mm  Precip %  Wind km/h
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-15  Partly cloudy                   21.6      16.2        0.0         6       10.2
2026-10-16  Overcast                        26.2      16.0        0.0         8        4.5
2026-10-17  Partly cloudy                   23.7      16.2        0.0         4        6.3

Date          UV max  UV level         Sunrise  Sunset  Daylight
----------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-15       3.5  Moderate           05:47   17:06    11h19m
2026-10-16       4.2  Moderate           05:48   17:05    11h17m
2026-10-17       5.3  Moderate           05:49   17:03    11h15m

Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.
```

At high latitudes the sunrise column reads `polar night` or `midnight sun` (with the sunset column blank) on days the sun doesn't rise or set.

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 weather_forecast.py "Chicago" --days 1 --json
```

```json
{
  "location": "Chicago",
  "admin1": "Illinois",
  "country": "United States",
  "latitude": 41.85003,
  "longitude": -87.65005,
  "unit": "°C",
  "days": [
    {
      "date": "2026-10-15",
      "weather_code": 51,
      "condition": "Light drizzle",
      "temp_max": 19.7,
      "temp_min": 13.5,
      "precipitation_sum": 0.4,
      "precipitation_probability_max": 61,
      "windspeed_max": 21.0,
      "uv_index_max": 0.95,
      "uv_category": "Low",
      "sunrise": "07:03",
      "sunset": "18:09",
      "daylight_hours": 11.1,
      "polar": null
    }
  ],
  "attribution": "Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0."
}
```

With `--json`, the leading progress line is suppressed and the result prints as a single JSON object on stdout. On failure, an exit code of `1` is returned and a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) is printed to stderr instead of plain text.

### How it works

1. **Geocode** — Calls `weather.geocode()` (shared with [`weather.py`](#weatherpy)) to resolve the free-text `LOCATION` to its top-matching place.
2. **Fetch the daily forecast** — Calls Open-Meteo's forecast endpoint with `daily=weathercode,temperature_2m_max,temperature_2m_min,precipitation_sum,precipitation_probability_max,windspeed_10m_max,uv_index_max,sunrise,sunset,daylight_duration`, `forecast_days=N`, `timezone=auto` (so daily buckets follow the location's own local calendar day), and the requested `--unit`.
3. **Decode and display** — Zips the parallel daily arrays into one record per day, maps each day's `weathercode` via `weather.weather_description()`, classifies the UV index with `uv_category()`, adds `weather.sun_fields()` (local sunrise/sunset, daylight hours, polar night/day), and prints the weather table and a UV-and-sun table (or the equivalent JSON object with `--json`).

### Configuration

These are set as constants near the top of the file — edit them directly to change behavior:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `DEFAULT_FORECAST_DAYS` | 5 | Number of days requested when `--days` isn't passed |
| `MAX_FORECAST_DAYS` | 16 | Upper bound accepted for `--days` (Open-Meteo's own limit) |
| `DAILY_FIELDS` | 10 fields (see above) | The Open-Meteo `daily=` parameter value |
| `UV_CATEGORIES` | WHO bands | `(lower bound, label)` pairs: 0 Low, 3 Moderate, 6 High, 8 Very high, 11 Extreme |

### API reference

- `fetch_daily_forecast(latitude, longitude, days=5, unit="celsius", start=None, end=None)` — Fetches the `daily` object for a coordinate pair: `days` days from today, or, given `start` and `end` (dates within the 16-day horizon), just that window ([`trip_weather.py`](#trip_weatherpy) uses it).
- `forecast_day(daily, i)` — Day `i` of that block as a day record (the shape `get_forecast()` returns); shared with `trip_weather.py`.
- `uv_category(uv)` — The WHO category for a daily maximum UV index (rounded half up first), or `None` when missing.
- `get_forecast(location, days=5, unit="celsius")` — Orchestrates geocoding (via `weather.geocode()`) and the daily-forecast fetch; returns `{location, admin1, country, latitude, longitude, unit, days}` where `days` is a list of `{date, weather_code, condition, temp_max, temp_min, precipitation_sum, precipitation_probability_max, windspeed_max, uv_index_max, uv_category, sunrise, sunset, daylight_hours, polar}` in chronological order, or `None` if the location can't be found.
- `format_forecast(row)` — Renders a `get_forecast()` record as two human-readable tables — date, condition, high, low, precipitation amount (mm), chance of rain (%) and max wind; then date, UV index and category, sunrise, sunset and daylight — with its place line from `weather.format_place()`.
- `_valid_days(value)` — argparse `type` for `--days`: an integer in `[1, MAX_FORECAST_DAYS]`; raises `argparse.ArgumentTypeError` otherwise.
- `parse_args(argv=None)` — Parses the positional `LOCATION` plus `--days`, `--unit`, and `--json`.
- `main(argv=None)` — Entry point; geocodes, fetches, handles top-level network/not-found errors, and prints the result (human-readable or JSON, depending on `--json`).

See [`weather.py`](#weatherpy) for `geocode()`, `fetch_json()`, `weather_description()`, `format_place()`, `daily_value()`, `sun_fields()`, `format_sun()` and `format_daylight()`.

### Error handling

- If either API request fails (network error, timeout, non-2xx response), the script prints an error and exits with status code 1 — plain text on stderr normally, or a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) on stderr when `--json` is passed.
- If the location can't be geocoded (no match), the script prints `Error: No location found matching '<LOCATION>'.` (or `{"error": "No location found matching '<LOCATION>'."}` with `--json`) to stderr and exits with status code 1.
- A `--days` value outside `1-16` is rejected by argument parsing itself (exit code 2), before any request is made.

### Notes / limitations

- **Attribution is required.** Open-Meteo's data is licensed [CC BY 4.0](https://open-meteo.com/en/terms), so the report always ends with `Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.` and the JSON carries the same text as `attribution` (the constant `weather.ATTRIBUTION`, shared by all four Open-Meteo scripts). Keep it when passing results on. Free use is for non-commercial purposes, under 10,000 calls a day.
- Same geocoding caveat as [`weather.py`](#weatherpy): the query's single top match is used, so an ambiguous name may resolve to the wrong place — disambiguate with a country or region in the query.
- One high/low/condition summary per calendar day — no hourly breakdown and no current-conditions reading (use [`weather.py`](#weatherpy) for that).
- `weathercode` is Open-Meteo's daily summary code for the day as a whole; a day with, say, morning rain and an otherwise clear afternoon is reported under a single condition.
- Forecast accuracy degrades toward the far end of the 16-day window, as with any weather model; Open-Meteo does not report a per-day confidence figure this script could surface.
- `uv_index_max` is the model's peak UV index for the day under the forecast cloud cover — clear-sky values can be higher. Sunrise and sunset are the location's local clock times.
- Results are a live snapshot; the forecast for a given date will change between runs as new model data comes in.

---

## Claude Code agent: `weather-forecast`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/weather-forecast.md`. It calls no API itself — it shells out to [`weather_forecast.py`](#weather_forecastpy) via the `Bash` tool and reports the result conversationally.

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer requests like "what's the forecast for Tokyo this week?", "will it rain in London tomorrow?", "5-day forecast for Chicago", "is it going to be hot in Singapore this weekend?", "how strong is the UV in Sydney today?" or "what time is sunset in Paris tomorrow?" by running the script and summarizing its JSON output, instead of guessing or using stale training data. For "what's the weather **right now**" questions (no forecast implied), Claude Code reaches for the [`weather`](#claude-code-agent-weather) agent instead, and for a past date the [`weather-history`](#claude-code-agent-weather-history) agent.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for upcoming/future weather questions about a named place — including the UV index, sunrise, sunset and daylight hours — based on the `description` field in its frontmatter.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "weather-forecast"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Passes the user's location text through to the tool mostly as-is, only adding a country/state qualifier itself when the name looks ambiguous and the user gave enough context to disambiguate.
2. Maps the user's phrasing to `--days` — "tomorrow" → 2 (reporting the second day), "this week" → 7, a specific count → that count, otherwise the script default of 5 — and to `--unit`, defaulting to `celsius` unless Fahrenheit is asked for or clearly expected.
3. Runs `python3 weather_forecast.py "LOCATION" [--days N] [--unit celsius|fahrenheit] --json` from the repo root.
4. Parses the JSON object's `days` array (`date`, `condition`, `temp_max`, `temp_min`, `precipitation_probability_max`, ...) and reports either a single day's condition/high-low/rain-chance or a compact multi-day table, calling out any day with notably high rain chance or a big temperature swing. For UV questions it gives the peak index and WHO category (with a sun-protection note from `High` up); for sunrise/sunset it gives the local times and notes polar night or midnight sun. If the command fails, it surfaces the `{"error": "..."}` from stderr — a "no location found" result is reported as-is rather than retried, while a network-looking failure is retried at most once.

### Configuration

The agent's frontmatter restricts it to the `Bash` tool only, since running the script and reading its stdout is all it needs.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`weather_forecast.py`](#weather_forecastpy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Ends its report with a credit line for Open-Meteo.com, whose data is licensed CC BY 4.0 and requires attribution.
- Requires `weather_forecast.py`, its `weather.py` dependency, and the `requests` package to be present and runnable from the repo root.
- Reports one high/low/condition summary per day, not an hourly breakdown, and has no current-conditions reading — for "right now" it defers to the [`weather`](#claude-code-agent-weather) agent rather than approximating from the forecast's first day.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`weather_forecast.py`](#weather_forecastpy) itself (geocoding takes only the top match for an ambiguous name, per-day summary codes, forecast accuracy tapering toward day 16, live-snapshot results).

---

## weather_history.py

Prints the past weather — condition, high/low/mean temperature, precipitation, snowfall, max wind, and sunrise/sunset/daylight — for a named location on a single date or over a range of up to 366 days, with a summary for a range (average high and low, warmest and coldest day, total precipitation, wet days, total snowfall). Uses [Open-Meteo](https://open-meteo.com/)'s public geocoding and [historical-weather (archive) API](https://open-meteo.com/en/docs/historical-weather-api), which covers every day from 1940-01-01 to today. No API key or authentication required.

Requires `weather.py` to be present in the same directory — it imports `geocode`, `fetch_json`, `place_fields`, `format_place`, `weather_description` and the daily-series helpers `daily_value`, `sun_fields`, `format_sun` and `format_daylight` directly from it. It also imports `die` from [`cli_utils.py`](#cli_utilspy).

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `weather.py` and `cli_utils.py` modules (no install — run from the repo root so they import)

### Usage

```bash
python3 weather_history.py LOCATION (--date YYYY-MM-DD | --start YYYY-MM-DD [--end YYYY-MM-DD] | --last N) [--unit {celsius,fahrenheit}] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `LOCATION` (positional) | — | Free-text place name, e.g. `"London"`, `"New York"`, `"Tromsø"` |
| `--date` | — | A single day. Cannot be combined with `--start` / `--end` |
| `--start` | — | First day of a range |
| `--end` | today | Last day of a range (needs `--start`) |
| `--last N` | — | The last `N` full days, ending yesterday — today is left out because its figures are still partial. `--last 1` is yesterday. Cannot be combined with `--date` / `--start` / `--end` |
| `--unit {celsius,fahrenheit}` | `celsius` | Temperature unit |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report |

One of `--date`, `--start` or `--last` is required. Dates must lie between 1940-01-01 and today, and a range can span at most 366 days.

#### Example output

```bash
python3 weather_history.py London --start 2026-01-05 --end 2026-01-09
```

```
Looking up the weather for 'London' on 2026-01-05 to 2026-01-09...

Weather for London, England, United Kingdom, 2026-01-05 to 2026-01-09

Date        Condition                   High °C   Low °C  Mean °C  Precip mm  Snow cm  Wind km/h      Sunrise  Sunset  Daylight
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-01-05  Overcast                        1.3     -3.7     -1.3        0.0      0.0       10.6        09:05   17:06     8h01m
2026-01-06  Slight snow fall                3.6     -5.2     -1.1        1.4     0.28       18.7        09:04   17:08     8h03m
2026-01-07  Heavy snow fall                 5.6      0.6      2.9        6.8     1.19       19.8        09:04   17:09     8h05m
2026-01-08  Moderate snow fall              6.8      1.3      3.5       13.5     0.21       23.3        09:04   17:10     8h07m
2026-01-09  Moderate drizzle                6.1      1.9      3.8        2.9      0.0       25.9        09:03   17:12     8h08m

Summary (5 days)
  Average high / low   4.7°C / -1.0°C
  Warmest day          6.8°C on 2026-01-08
  Coldest night        -5.2°C on 2026-01-06
  Total precipitation  24.6 mm (4 wet days, >= 1 mm)
  Total snowfall       1.7 cm

Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.
```

At high latitudes the sunrise column reads `polar night` or `midnight sun` (with the sunset column blank) on days the sun doesn't rise or set.

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 weather_history.py "New York" --date 2010-12-25 --json
```

```json
{
  "location": "New York",
  "admin1": "New York",
  "country": "United States",
  "latitude": 40.71427,
  "longitude": -74.00597,
  "start_date": "2010-12-25",
  "end_date": "2010-12-25",
  "unit": "°C",
  "days": [
    {
      "date": "2010-12-25",
      "weather_code": 3,
      "condition": "Overcast",
      "temp_max": 0.9,
      "temp_min": -4.0,
      "temp_mean": -1.7,
      "precipitation_sum": 0.0,
      "rain_sum": 0.0,
      "snowfall_sum": 0.0,
      "windspeed_max": 9.3,
      "sunrise": "08:18",
      "sunset": "17:34",
      "daylight_hours": 9.26,
      "polar": null
    }
  ],
  "summary": null,
  "attribution": "Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0."
}
```

For a range, `summary` is `{days, days_with_data, avg_temp_max, avg_temp_min, warmest: {date, temp_max}, coldest: {date, temp_min}, total_precipitation, wet_days, total_snowfall}`. Precipitation and rain are in mm, snowfall in cm, wind in km/h; any value the archive doesn't have is `null`.

### How it works

1. **Validate the window** — `resolve_window()` turns `--date` / `--start` / `--end` / `--last` into a `(start, end)` pair during argument parsing (`--last N` → yesterday minus `N - 1` days to yesterday), rejecting a missing, combined, reversed, pre-1940, future or over-366-day window with exit code 2 before any request.
2. **Geocode** — `weather.geocode()` resolves `LOCATION` to its top match.
3. **Fetch** — Calls `archive-api.open-meteo.com/v1/archive` with `start_date` / `end_date`, `daily=weathercode,temperature_2m_max,temperature_2m_min,temperature_2m_mean,precipitation_sum,rain_sum,snowfall_sum,windspeed_10m_max,sunrise,sunset,daylight_duration`, `timezone=auto` (so days follow the location's own calendar) and the requested `--unit`.
4. **Build days** — `build_day()` reads each day's values with `weather.daily_value()` (missing → `None`), maps the weather code via `weather_description()`, and adds `weather.sun_fields()` (local `HH:MM` sunrise/sunset, daylight hours, polar night/day).
5. **Summarize** — `summarize()` averages the highs and lows, finds the warmest and coldest days, and totals precipitation, wet days (≥ 1 mm) and snowfall, leaving out days missing that value.
6. **Report** — the table and summary, or the JSON object.

### Configuration

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `ARCHIVE_URL` | `https://archive-api.open-meteo.com/v1/archive` | Open-Meteo historical-weather endpoint |
| `EARLIEST_DATE` | `1940-01-01` | The archive's first day; earlier dates are rejected |
| `MAX_RANGE_DAYS` | 366 | Longest range accepted, to keep the table readable |
| `WET_DAY_MM` | 1.0 | Precipitation (mm) at or above which a day counts as wet |
| `DAILY_FIELDS` | 11 fields (see above) | The Open-Meteo `daily=` parameter value |

### API reference

- `fetch_daily_history(latitude, longitude, start, end, unit="celsius")` — Fetches the archive's `daily` object for a coordinate pair and `datetime.date` range.
- `build_day(daily, i)` — Day `i` of a `daily` block as a record.
- `summarize(days)` — The range summary, or `None` for a single day or a range with no data.
- `get_history(location, start, end, unit="celsius")` — Orchestrates geocoding, the fetch, day records and summary; returns `{location, admin1, country, latitude, longitude, start_date, end_date, unit, days, summary}`, or `None` if the location can't be found.
- `format_history(row)` — Renders a `get_history()` record as the table plus summary.
- `resolve_window(args, parser, today=None)` — Turns the parsed date flags into `(start, end)`, calling `parser.error()` (exit 2) for an invalid window.
- `parse_args(argv=None, today=None)`, `main(argv=None)` — The CLI; `today` is injectable for tests.

See [`weather.py`](#weatherpy) for `geocode()`, `fetch_json()`, `daily_value()`, `sun_fields()`, `format_sun()` and `format_daylight()`.

### Error handling

- An invalid window — no `--date`, `--start` or `--last`, `--date` combined with `--start`/`--end`, `--last` combined with any of them, a `--last` that isn't a positive integer, `--end` without `--start`, `--end` before `--start`, a date before 1940-01-01 or after today, or a range over 366 days — is rejected by argument parsing (exit code 2), before any request. A future date's message points to `weather_forecast.py`.
- If a request fails, the script exits 1 with the error on stderr (`{"error": "..."}` with `--json`). When Open-Meteo rejects a request it explains why, and that reason is the error message.
- If the location can't be geocoded, it exits 1 with `No location found matching '<LOCATION>'.`

### Notes / limitations

- **Attribution is required.** Open-Meteo's data is licensed [CC BY 4.0](https://open-meteo.com/en/terms), so the report always ends with `Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.` and the JSON carries the same text as `attribution` (the constant `weather.ATTRIBUTION`, shared by all four Open-Meteo scripts). Keep it when passing results on. Free use is for non-commercial purposes, under 10,000 calls a day.
- The data is a **reanalysis** — a weather model run over past observations on a grid of roughly 10-25 km — not a single weather station's record, so it can differ from a nearby station, especially on coasts and in mountains. The latest days come from a faster model and are revised for about a week; today's values are partial.
- `weathercode` is the model's daily summary code, derived from its precipitation and cloud; a day's "Heavy snow fall" can sit alongside a modest snowfall total.
- Same geocoding caveat as [`weather.py`](#weatherpy): the top match is used. Accents matter — `"Tromso"` resolves to a different place than `"Tromsø"`.
- Sunrise and sunset are the location's local clock times, from the same time zone Open-Meteo resolves for the coordinates.

---

## Claude Code agent: `weather-history`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/weather-history.md`. It calls no API itself — it shells out to [`weather_history.py`](#weather_historypy) via the `Bash` tool and reports the result conversationally.

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer requests like "what was the weather in London on 1 January 2020?", "how much did it rain in Singapore last week?", "how hot was Tokyo last August?" or "was it snowing in New York on Christmas Day 2010?" from Open-Meteo's archive, instead of guessing.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for weather, temperature, rainfall, snowfall or sunrise/sunset questions about a past date or period at a named place, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter. Current conditions go to [`weather`](#claude-code-agent-weather) and upcoming days to [`weather-forecast`](#claude-code-agent-weather-forecast).
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "weather-history"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Passes the location through mostly as the user wrote it, keeping accents, and adds a country/state qualifier only for an ambiguous name.
2. Turns the user's wording into `--date` or `--start`/`--end` relative to today, or `--last N` for "the past N days" (the N full days ending yesterday, since today's figures are partial); for a span over 366 days it runs the tool once per year and combines the results, saying so.
3. Runs `python3 weather_history.py "LOCATION" (--date … | --start … [--end …]) [--unit …] --json` from the repo root.
4. Leads with the single day's condition and high/low, or with the range summary (average high/low, warmest and coldest days, total precipitation, wet days) and a compact day table for up to about two weeks. It notes that the figures are gridded model data rather than a station record and that today's are partial. An exit code 2 means it fixes the dates rather than retrying; a "no location found" result is reported as-is; a network-looking failure is retried at most once.

### Configuration

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`weather_history.py`](#weather_historypy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Ends its report with a credit line for Open-Meteo.com, whose data is licensed CC BY 4.0 and requires attribution.
- Requires `weather_history.py`, its `weather.py` / `cli_utils.py` dependencies, and the `requests` package to be runnable from the repo root.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`weather_history.py`](#weather_historypy): gridded reanalysis rather than station data, provisional recent days, a 366-day range limit, top-match geocoding.

---

## air_quality.py

Prints the current air quality — US AQI, European AQI, raw pollutant concentrations, and pollen counts (Europe only) — for a named location, or with `--forecast` each coming day's worst hour of US AQI, European AQI and pollen, using [Open-Meteo](https://open-meteo.com/)'s public geocoding and air quality APIs. No API key or authentication required.

Requires `weather.py` to be present in the same directory — it imports `fetch_json`, `format_place`, and `geocode` directly from it rather than shelling out or duplicating that logic (the same sharing pattern [`weather_forecast.py`](#weather_forecastpy) uses). It also imports `die` from [`cli_utils.py`](#cli_utilspy).

Reports both the US AQI (EPA scale — the "AQI" most commonly referenced in US media and apps) and the European AQI (EAQI scale) side by side. The two indices use different breakpoints and are **not directly comparable** — a European AQI of 49 and a US AQI of 132 for the same air can both be correct.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `weather.py` and `cli_utils.py` modules (no install — run from the repo root so they import)

```bash
pip install requests
```

### Usage

```bash
python3 air_quality.py LOCATION [--forecast] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `LOCATION` (positional) | — | Free-text place name, e.g. `"London"`, `"New York"`, `"Tokyo"` |
| `--forecast` | off | Report each coming day's worst hour (US AQI and category, European AQI, pollen) instead of current conditions; values run about 4–5 days ahead (pollen about 3, Europe only) |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report — for calling this script as a tool from an agent or another program |

#### Example output

```
Looking up air quality for 'Singapore'...

Air quality for Singapore, Singapore
  US AQI: 132 (Unhealthy for Sensitive Groups)
  European AQI: 49
  PM2.5: 30.2 µg/m³
  PM10: 35.0 µg/m³
  Carbon monoxide: 388.0 µg/m³
  Nitrogen dioxide: 12.1 µg/m³
  Sulphur dioxide: 6.8 µg/m³
  Ozone: 99.0 µg/m³
  Pollen: not available (Europe only)
  As of: 2026-09-15T14:00

Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.
```

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 air_quality.py "Beijing" --json
```

```json
{
  "location": "Beijing",
  "admin1": "Beijing Municipality",
  "country": "China",
  "latitude": 39.9075,
  "longitude": 116.39723,
  "us_aqi": 152,
  "us_aqi_category": "Unhealthy",
  "european_aqi": 59,
  "pm2_5": 44.0,
  "pm10": 54.8,
  "carbon_monoxide": 582.0,
  "nitrogen_dioxide": 58.7,
  "sulphur_dioxide": 11.2,
  "ozone": 70.0,
  "alder_pollen": null,
  "birch_pollen": null,
  "grass_pollen": null,
  "mugwort_pollen": null,
  "olive_pollen": null,
  "ragweed_pollen": null,
  "time": "2026-09-15T14:00",
  "attribution": "Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0."
}
```

With `--json`, the leading progress line is suppressed and the result prints as a single JSON object on stdout. On failure, an exit code of `1` is returned and a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) is printed to stderr instead of plain text.

For a place in Europe, the pollen line lists each plant's count, e.g. `Pollen (grains/m³): alder 0.0, birch 0.0, grass 0.0, mugwort 0.0, olive 0.0, ragweed 0.0` (London in October, out of season).

#### Forecast (`--forecast`)

From a live run on 2026-10-21:

```
$ python3 air_quality.py Delhi --forecast
Looking up the air quality forecast for 'Delhi'...

Air quality forecast for Delhi, National Capital Territory of Delhi, India (each day's worst hour)

Date          US AQI  Category                          EU AQI  Pollen (grains/m³)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-21       176  Unhealthy                            128  -
2026-10-22       187  Unhealthy                            141  -
2026-10-23       185  Unhealthy                            132  -
2026-10-24       170  Unhealthy                            122  -
2026-10-25       167  Unhealthy                             91  -  [19 h of forecast]

Note: Forecast values run out after about 4-5 days (pollen after about 3, and only in Europe).

Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.
```

Each value is the day's **worst hour**. A day the forecast covers only partly is marked with its hours; pollen is `-` outside Europe or past the pollen forecast, `none` when every plant is at zero. With `--json` the result is `{location, admin1, country, latitude, longitude, days: [{date, us_aqi, us_aqi_category, european_aqi, alder_pollen … ragweed_pollen, hours}], note, attribution}`.

### How it works

1. **Geocode** — Calls `weather.geocode()` (shared with [`weather.py`](#weatherpy)) to resolve the free-text `LOCATION` to its top-matching place.
2. **Fetch current air quality** — Calls Open-Meteo's air quality endpoint (`air-quality-api.open-meteo.com/v1/air-quality`) with `current=us_aqi,european_aqi,pm2_5,pm10,carbon_monoxide,nitrogen_dioxide,sulphur_dioxide,ozone` plus the six `<plant>_pollen` fields (`CURRENT_FIELDS`) and `timezone=auto` at that location's coordinates.
3. **Categorize and display** — Maps the numeric US AQI to its EPA category label via `us_aqi_category()` (`US_AQI_CATEGORIES`), and prints location, both AQI values, the raw pollutant concentrations, the pollen counts on one line (via `format_pollen()`), and the observation time (or the equivalent JSON object with `--json`).
4. **With `--forecast`** — instead of steps 2–3, `get_air_quality_forecast()` fetches the hourly forecast for every forecast day from the place's today (`fetch_hourly_air_quality()`), reduces it to each day's worst hour (`daily_maxima()`), and `format_forecast()` prints the table (shared with [`trip_weather.py`](#trip_weatherpy) as `forecast_table()`) with the forecast note.

### Configuration

These are set as constants near the top of the file — edit them directly to change behavior:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `POLLEN_TYPES` | `alder`, `birch`, `grass`, `mugwort`, `olive`, `ragweed` | Plants to report pollen for; each becomes a `<plant>_pollen` field (`POLLEN_FIELDS`) |
| `CURRENT_FIELDS` | `us_aqi,european_aqi,pm2_5,pm10,carbon_monoxide,nitrogen_dioxide,sulphur_dioxide,ozone` + `POLLEN_FIELDS` | The Open-Meteo `current=` parameter value |
| `US_AQI_CATEGORIES` | 6 EPA bands, `Good` through `Hazardous` | Upper-bound/label pairs used by `us_aqi_category()` |
| `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` | 10 (from `weather.py`) | Per-request timeout in seconds |

### API reference

- `us_aqi_category(us_aqi)` — Maps a US AQI value to its EPA category label (`Good`, `Moderate`, `Unhealthy for Sensitive Groups`, `Unhealthy`, `Very Unhealthy`, `Hazardous`); returns `None` if `us_aqi` is `None`.
- `fetch_current_air_quality(latitude, longitude)` — Fetches the `current` object for a coordinate pair from Open-Meteo's air quality endpoint.
- `fetch_hourly_air_quality(latitude, longitude, start=None, end=None)` — Fetches the hourly air-quality forecast (`HOURLY_FIELDS`: US and European AQI and pollen): between two dates when given (as [`trip_weather.py`](#trip_weatherpy) asks), otherwise all `MAX_FORECAST_DAYS` (7) days from the place's today. Values run out after about 4–5 days (pollen about 3).
- `daily_maxima(hourly)` — Reduces that hourly block to each local day's worst hour: `{date: {us_aqi, us_aqi_category, european_aqi, <plant>_pollen…, hours}}`, `hours` counting the hours with a US AQI; days with no values are left out.
- `get_air_quality_forecast(location)` — Geocodes and returns `{location, admin1, country, latitude, longitude, days, note}` for `--forecast`, `days` in date order, or `None` for an unknown place.
- `format_day_pollen(air)` — A day's pollen as `birch 3.2, grass 1`, `none` (all zero) or `-` (no data).
- `forecast_table(dated)` — The forecast table's lines for `(date, daily_maxima() entry)` pairs, marking partial days; shared with `trip_weather.py`.
- `format_forecast(row)` — Renders a `get_air_quality_forecast()` record: title, table (or "No forecast values returned.") and the `FORECAST_NOTE`.
- `get_air_quality(location)` — Orchestrates geocoding (via `weather.geocode()`) and the current-air-quality fetch; returns the flattened `{location, admin1, country, latitude, longitude, us_aqi, us_aqi_category, european_aqi, pm2_5, pm10, carbon_monoxide, nitrogen_dioxide, sulphur_dioxide, ozone, alder_pollen, birch_pollen, grass_pollen, mugwort_pollen, olive_pollen, ragweed_pollen, time}` record, or `None` if the location can't be found.
- `format_pollen(row)` — Renders a record's pollen counts as one `Pollen (grains/m³): alder …, birch …` line, or `Pollen: not available (Europe only)` when every pollen value is `None` or missing.
- `format_air_quality(row)` — Renders a `get_air_quality()` record as a multi-line human-readable string (its place line comes from `weather.format_place()`).
- `parse_args(argv=None)` — Parses the positional `LOCATION` plus `--forecast` and `--json`.
- `main(argv=None)` — Entry point; with `--forecast` uses `get_air_quality_forecast()` / `format_forecast()`, otherwise geocodes, fetches, handles top-level network/not-found errors, and prints the result (human-readable or JSON, depending on `--json`).

See [`weather.py`](#weatherpy) for `geocode()`, `fetch_json()`, and `format_place()`.

### Error handling

- If either API request fails (network error, timeout, non-2xx response), the script prints an error and exits with status code 1 — plain text on stderr normally, or a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) on stderr when `--json` is passed.
- If the location can't be geocoded (no match), the script prints `Error: No location found matching '<LOCATION>'.` (or `{"error": "No location found matching '<LOCATION>'."}` with `--json`) to stderr and exits with status code 1.

### Notes / limitations

- **Attribution is required.** Open-Meteo's data is licensed [CC BY 4.0](https://open-meteo.com/en/terms), so the report always ends with `Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.` and the JSON carries the same text as `attribution` (the constant `weather.ATTRIBUTION`, shared by all four Open-Meteo scripts). Keep it when passing results on. Free use is for non-commercial purposes, under 10,000 calls a day.
- Same geocoding caveat as [`weather.py`](#weatherpy): the query's single top match is used, so an ambiguous name may resolve to the wrong place — disambiguate by adding a country or region to the query.
- Without `--forecast`, reports only the current reading at a single instant. `--forecast` gives each day's worst hour, not hourly detail or averages, and reaches only about 4–5 days ahead (pollen about 3). There is no historical air quality (Open-Meteo has archived history; this script doesn't call it).
- The US AQI and European AQI are **not directly comparable** — they use different pollutant breakpoints and scales (US AQI runs roughly 0-500, European AQI roughly 0-100+). Don't average or otherwise combine them.
- Open-Meteo's air quality model (CAMS) is coarser-resolution than a dedicated ground-station network like WAQI/AirNow, so readings can differ somewhat from a country's own official monitoring stations, especially for hyper-local pollution sources.
- Pollen counts come from Open-Meteo's CAMS European model, so they exist **only for Europe** — everywhere else all six are `null`. In Europe a `0.0` is a real reading, usually meaning the plant is out of season. They are raw counts in grains/m³; the script doesn't map them to low/moderate/high bands.
- Results are a live snapshot; conditions will differ between runs.

---

## Claude Code agent: `air-quality`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/air-quality.md`. It calls no API itself — it shells out to [`air_quality.py`](#air_qualitypy) via the `Bash` tool and reports the result conversationally.

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer questions like "what's the air quality in Beijing?", "is the air bad in Singapore right now?", "AQI for Delhi", "how polluted is it in Los Angeles today?", or "what's the pollen count in London?" by running the script and summarizing its JSON output, instead of guessing or using stale training data.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for current or coming days' air pollution / AQI / smog / pollen questions about a named place (adding `--forecast` for coming days), based on the `description` field in its frontmatter.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "air-quality"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Passes the user's location text through to the tool mostly as-is, only adding a country/state qualifier itself when the name looks ambiguous and the user gave enough context to disambiguate.
2. Runs `python3 air_quality.py "LOCATION" --json` from the repo root.
3. Parses the JSON object (`us_aqi`, `us_aqi_category`, `european_aqi`, `pm2_5`, `pm10`, `carbon_monoxide`, `nitrogen_dioxide`, `sulphur_dioxide`, `ozone`, the six `<plant>_pollen` counts, `time`) and leads with the US AQI and its category, mentioning the European AQI only when relevant and never combining the two scales. It calls out a specific pollutant only if the user asked for detail or one is notably elevated, and reports pollen only when asked about pollen, hay fever or allergies — saying it isn't available (rather than zero) when every count is `null` outside Europe. If the command fails, it surfaces the `{"error": "..."}` from stderr — a "no location found" result is reported as-is rather than retried, while a network-looking failure is retried at most once.

### Configuration

The agent's frontmatter restricts it to the `Bash` tool only, since running the script and reading its stdout is all it needs.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`air_quality.py`](#air_qualitypy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Ends its report with a credit line for Open-Meteo.com, whose data is licensed CC BY 4.0 and requires attribution.
- Requires `air_quality.py` (and its `weather.py` and `requests` dependencies) to be present and runnable from the repo root.
- Pollen counts exist only for places in Europe, and are raw grains/m³ with no low/high bands.
- Current conditions, or with `--forecast` the next 4–5 days (each day's worst hour); there is no historical air quality, so it says so rather than fabricating one.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`air_quality.py`](#air_qualitypy) itself (geocoding takes only the top match for an ambiguous name, the two AQI scales aren't comparable, Open-Meteo's model resolution versus dedicated ground stations, live-snapshot results).

---

## trip_weather.py

Weather for a trip: one place and a range of dates, merged by date from the other Open-Meteo scripts. Days within the 16-day forecast get [`weather_forecast.py`](#weather_forecastpy)'s forecast (condition, high / low, rain and its chance, UV, sunrise, sunset, daylight). Days further ahead get **typical weather**: the same calendar dates in each of the last few years from [`weather_history.py`](#weather_historypy)'s archive, averaged and clearly labelled as not a forecast. Days within the air-quality forecast also get [`air_quality.py`](#air_qualitypy)'s worst hour of US AQI, European AQI and pollen. No API key required.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `weather.py`, `weather_forecast.py`, `weather_history.py`, `air_quality.py` and `cli_utils.py` (run from the repo root)

### Usage

```bash
python3 trip_weather.py LOCATION --start YYYY-MM-DD [--end YYYY-MM-DD] [--years N] [--unit celsius|fahrenheit] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `LOCATION` (positional) | — | Place name, e.g. `"Paris"`, `"Kyoto"` |
| `--start` | — (required) | First day of the trip; from yesterday (time zones) up to a year ahead |
| `--end` | `--start` | Last day of the trip; at most 31 days in all |
| `--years N` | 5 | Past years averaged for typical weather (1–20) |
| `--unit` | `celsius` | `celsius` or `fahrenheit` |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report |

#### Example output

From a live run on 2026-10-21 (abridged):

```
Trip weather for Paris, Île-de-France Region, France, 2026-10-23 to 2026-11-12

Forecast
Date        Condition                   High °C   Low °C  Precip mm  Precip %    UV  UV level        Sunrise  Sunset  Daylight
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-23  Slight rain                    13.7      9.9       4.72        91   0.8  Low               08:23   18:45    10h23m
2026-10-24  Slight rain                    13.9      8.4       12.7        91   0.9  Low               08:24   18:44    10h20m
…
2026-11-04  Light drizzle                  11.9      8.2       1.05         -   1.3  Low               08:42   18:25     9h44m

Typical weather, from the same dates in 2021-2025 (not a forecast)
Date          High °C        (range)   Low °C        (range)  Precip mm  Wet years      Sunrise  Sunset  Daylight
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-11-05       13.2 (11.1 to 16.8)      7.3  (4.6 to 10.3)        0.6     2 of 5        08:44   18:24     9h40m
2026-11-06       12.5 (10.9 to 14.3)      7.2  (0.5 to 11.2)        4.1     3 of 5        08:45   18:22     9h37m
…

Air quality (the day's worst hour)
Date          US AQI  Category                          EU AQI  Pollen (grains/m³)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-23        34  Good                                  28  none
2026-10-24        34  Good                                  26  none
2026-10-25        29  Good                                  34  -  [16 h of forecast]

Note: 2026-11-05 onwards is beyond the forecast: typical weather for those dates in 2021-2025, not a forecast.
Note: Air quality is only forecast about 4-5 days ahead (pollen about 3, and only in Europe); later days have none.

Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0.
```

Pollen shows the plants above zero, `none` when all are zero, and `-` when there is no pollen data (outside Europe, or past the pollen forecast).

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```json
{
  "location": "Paris", "admin1": "Île-de-France Region", "country": "France",
  "latitude": 48.85341, "longitude": 2.3488, "unit": "°C",
  "start_date": "2026-11-03", "end_date": "2026-11-05",
  "typical_years": [2021, 2025],
  "days": [
    {"date": "2026-11-03", "source": "forecast", "weather_code": 51, "condition": "Light drizzle", "temp_max": 13.8, "temp_min": 10.6, "precipitation_sum": 2.0, "precipitation_probability_max": 25, "windspeed_max": 14.3, "uv_index_max": 2.1, "uv_category": "Low", "sunrise": "08:40", "sunset": "18:27", "daylight_hours": 9.78, "polar": null, "air": null},
    {"date": "2026-11-05", "source": "typical", "years": 5, "temp_max": 13.2, "temp_min": 7.3, "temp_max_range": [11.1, 16.8], "temp_min_range": [4.6, 10.3], "precipitation_sum": 0.6, "wet_years": 2, "snowfall_sum": 0.0, "sunrise": "08:44", "sunset": "18:24", "daylight_hours": 9.67, "polar": null, "air": null}
  ],
  "summary": {"forecast_days": 2, "typical_days": 1, "air_quality_days": 0, "forecast_wet_days": 2, "highest": {"date": "2026-11-03", "temp_max": 13.8, "source": "forecast"}, "lowest": {"date": "2026-11-05", "temp_min": 7.3, "source": "typical"}},
  "notes": ["2026-11-05 onwards is beyond the forecast: typical weather for those dates in 2021-2025, not a forecast.", "Air quality is only forecast about 4-5 days ahead (pollen about 3, and only in Europe); later days have none."],
  "attribution": "Data by Open-Meteo.com (https://open-meteo.com/), licensed under CC BY 4.0."
}
```

(Abridged: one day of each kind.) A day with air quality has `air`: `{us_aqi, us_aqi_category, european_aqi, alder_pollen … ragweed_pollen, hours}`, each the day's maximum, with `hours` the hours that had a US AQI.

### How it works

1. **Check the dates** (`parse_args()`): the trip starts no earlier than yesterday in UTC (a place's "today" can be a day behind), ends within 366 days, and is at most 31 days long; anything else exits with status 2 before any request.
2. **Geocode** the place once with `weather.geocode()`.
3. **Forecast** (`forecast_records()`): days up to today + 15 are fetched in one request with `weather_forecast.fetch_daily_forecast(start=…, end=…)` and built with `weather_forecast.forecast_day()`. A day the forecast returns empty (seen on the 16th day) is treated as beyond it.
4. **Typical weather** (`typical_records()`): for the remaining days, one archive request per past year (`weather_history.fetch_daily_history()` over the same dates, `shift_year()` moving 29 February to the 28th) and, per date, `typical_record()`: average high and low, their range, average precipitation, wet years (at least 1 mm), average snowfall, and last year's sun times.
5. **Air quality**: if the trip starts within 7 days, `air_quality.fetch_hourly_air_quality()` over the trip's first days, reduced by `air_quality.daily_maxima()` to each day's worst hour.
6. **Merge and summarize** by date, add the notes, and print the three tables (or the JSON), ending with the Open-Meteo credit.

### Configuration

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `MAX_TRIP_DAYS` | 31 | Longest trip |
| `MAX_DAYS_AHEAD` | 366 | How far ahead a trip may end |
| `DEFAULT_YEARS` / `MAX_YEARS` | 5 / 20 | `--years` default and maximum |
| `FORECAST_DAYS` | 16 | From `weather_forecast.MAX_FORECAST_DAYS` |
| `WET_DAY_MM` | 1.0 | From `weather_history.WET_DAY_MM` |

### API reference

- `get_trip_weather(location, start, end, years=5, unit="celsius", today=None)` — the whole record, or `None` for an unknown place.
- `forecast_records(latitude, longitude, dates, unit)` / `typical_records(latitude, longitude, dates, years, unit)` / `typical_record(date, samples)` — the two kinds of day record.
- `summarize(days)`, `format_trip(row)` — summary and report (the air-quality table comes from `air_quality.forecast_table()`).
- `shift_year(date, years)`, `date_range(start, end)`, `today_utc()` — date helpers.

### Error handling

- A place that can't be geocoded exits with status 1 (`No location found matching '…'`); so does a network or API error. With `--json` the error is `{"error": "..."}` on stderr.
- A malformed date, an end before the start, a trip over 31 days, a start in the past, an end more than a year ahead, or `--years` outside 1–20 exits with status 2, before any request.

### Notes / limitations

- **Typical weather is history, not a forecast**: a five-year average says what is usual, not what will happen, and the report labels it that way everywhere. It has no condition or UV (the archive has no UV), and its sun times are last year's.
- Typical weather costs one archive request per past year (5 by default), on top of the geocoding, forecast and air-quality requests.
- The air-quality forecast runs out after about 4–5 days and pollen after about 3 (Europe only), so most trips have air quality only for their first days, or none.
- The US and European AQI use different scales; they are reported side by side, never combined.
- Inherits the other weather scripts' limits: geocoding takes the top match for an ambiguous name, and days follow the place's local calendar.

---

## Claude Code agent: `trip-weather`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/trip-weather.md`. It calls no API itself — it shells out to [`trip_weather.py`](#trip_weatherpy) via the `Bash` tool and reports the result conversationally.

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer "what's Paris like next week?", "weather for my trip to Kyoto from 3 to 10 November", "what should I pack for Lisbon next month?" or "what's Reykjavik usually like over Christmas?" with the forecast where there is one, typical weather beyond it, and UV, sun times, air quality and pollen.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects it for questions about the weather for a trip, a stay or travel dates at a place, especially more than about two weeks away. The next few days stay with `weather-forecast`, right now with `weather`, and past dates with `weather-history`.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "trip-weather"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Turns the user's dates ("next week", "the first week of June", "over Christmas") into `--start` / `--end` and says which dates it used; a stay over 31 days is split or narrowed.
2. Runs `python3 trip_weather.py "LOCATION" --start … --end … --json`.
3. Leads with the overall picture (range of highs and lows, how rainy, anything notable), then the forecast days, then the typical days, always labelled as typical weather over the named years and not a forecast, then air quality and pollen where forecast, and ends with the Open-Meteo credit.

### Configuration

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`trip_weather.py`](#trip_weatherpy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Requires `trip_weather.py` and the weather modules it imports (plus `requests`) to be runnable from the repo root.
- Being a prompt rather than code, the agent isn't covered by the offline test suite. [`docs/features/agent/trip-weather/test-runs.md`](docs/features/agent/trip-weather/test-runs.md) lists targeted live runs of its judgement rules ("next week", forecast plus typical, a stay over 31 days, past dates, packing advice with air quality, °F); all passed on 2026-10-21 and found the daylight-saving error in sun times that `weather.local_daily()` now fixes.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`trip_weather.py`](#trip_weatherpy) itself (typical weather is history, short air-quality forecast, 31-day trips).
