It covers how bumpy the prices were and how they moved together; the direction of a single instrument is the [`stock-trend`](#claude-code-agent-stock-trend) agent's job.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for questions about the volatility, drawdown, swings, riskiness, or correlation of named companies, tickers, indices, exchange rates, crypto coins or commodities, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "stock-risk"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Resolves each company to a Yahoo-notation ticker, adding the exchange suffix for non-US listings (`.L`, `.DE`, `.NS`, …), or, for a non-stock, Yahoo's own symbol — an index (`^GSPC`), currency pair (`EURUSD=X`) or crypto coin (`BTC-USD`); Singapore, Tokyo and futures are declined for now; if a ticker is uncertain it says so rather than guessing.
2. Adds `--benchmark` when the user compares against a market, or asks how risky one stock is and the stock's home index makes a useful reference; a comparison of named instruments lists them all as symbols instead.
3. Chooses the window from the user's phrasing — `--range 3mo` for "lately" / "this quarter", `--range ytd` / `1y` / `5y` for "this year" / "past year" / "past 5 years", `--last N` for "past N days/weeks", `--start`/`--end` for a named month or span, otherwise the default `--range 1y`.
4. Runs `python3 stock_risk.py SYMBOL [SYMBOL ...] [--benchmark SYMBOL] <window flags> --json` from the repo root and parses `{symbols, benchmark, correlations, skipped}`.
5. Answers the question asked first, then gives volatility as a yearly percentage (against the benchmark when there is one), the worst fall with its dates and recovery, the distance from the high when relevant, and each correlation with a plain reading; a table for three or more symbols. Skipped symbols are named with their reason. It closes with a caveat that these figures describe the past window, don't predict future swings, aren't causation, and aren't investment advice. On failure it surfaces the `{"error": "..."}` from stderr and retries at most once.

### Configuration

The agent's frontmatter restricts it to `Bash` (run the script).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`stock_risk.py`](#stock_riskpy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Requires `stock_risk.py` and its `price_source.py`, `yahoo_finance.py`, `indicators.py` and `cli_utils.py` helper modules (plus the `requests` dependency and the API keys) to be present and runnable from the repo root.
- Only as good as the tickers it picks — a wrong or ambiguous symbol yields the wrong company's figures or a skipped symbol.
- The plain readings it gives (the rough scale for volatility, the bands for correlation) are rules of thumb written into the agent, not properties of the data.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`stock_risk.py`](#stock_riskpy) itself (descriptive figures only, sensitive to the window, free-plan history limits, the weekend heuristic for annualization).

---

## yahoo_finance.py

Shared helper module for [`stock_price_history.py`](#stock_price_historypy), [`stock_candlestick_chart.py`](#stock_candlestick_chartpy), [`stock_rebased_chart.py`](#stock_rebased_chartpy) and [`stock_risk.py`](#stock_riskpy) (for the window flags and chart-result parsing: since 2026-10-21 their data comes from [`price_source.py`](#price_sourcepy), which returns the same shape); [`market_top_volume.py`](#market_top_volumepy) uses its cookie + crumb handshake; [`stock_intrinsic_value.py`](#stock_intrinsic_valuepy), [`stock_earnings_calendar.py`](#stock_earnings_calendarpy), [`stock_analyst_targets.py`](#stock_analyst_targetspy) and [`stock_holders.py`](#stock_holderspy) its `quoteSummary` fetch built on that handshake (as does [`stock_fundamental.py`](#stock_fundamentalpy), through `stock_intrinsic_value.py`'s fetch); and [`app/stock_information/`](#appstock_information) its fetch for the quote. It holds everything those scripts had in common — the Yahoo Finance endpoint constants, the `--range`/`--last`/`--start`/`--end` flags and the query-window builder, the HTTP call, the raw-payload navigation, exchange-local session dates, the crumb handshake, and the `quoteSummary` fetch and value readers. Each script keeps its own row shaping (rounding, date type, which rows to drop) and its own output stage. Not a CLI — it is imported, not run.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `cli_utils.py` (for `positive_int`, used by `add_window_args()`) — run from the repo root so it imports

### Constants

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `CHART_URL` | `…/v8/finance/chart/{symbol}` | Yahoo chart-endpoint URL template |
| `HEADERS` | browser `User-Agent` | Required — Yahoo rejects requests without a browser-like User-Agent |
| `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` | 15 | Request timeout in seconds |
| `VALID_RANGES` | `["5d", "1mo", …, "max"]` | Accepted `--range` keywords (Yahoo's own range vocabulary) |
| `SECONDS_PER_DAY` | 86400 | Used to pad an explicit `--end` by a day so the final bar is inclusive |
| `COOKIE_URLS` | `("https://fc.yahoo.com", "https://finance.yahoo.com")` | Pages tried in order to pick up Yahoo's consent cookie |
| `CRUMB_URL` | `…/v1/test/getcrumb` | Crumb endpoint for the screener / `quoteSummary` calls |
| `QUOTE_SUMMARY_HOSTS` | `query2` then `query1` `…/v10/finance/quoteSummary/{symbol}` | `quoteSummary` URL templates, tried in order |

### API reference

- `parse_date(value)` — Parses a `YYYY-MM-DD` string to a UTC `datetime`, raising `ValueError` with a clear message on a bad format.
- `add_window_args(parser, default_range, last_help)` — Adds the shared window flags to an argparse parser: a mutually exclusive `--range` (choices `VALID_RANGES`, default `default_range`) / `--last N` (`cli_utils.positive_int`, help text `last_help`), plus `--start` / `--end`. Used by all four price scripts.
- `session_datetime(timestamp, gmtoffset)` — Converts a Yahoo UTC epoch timestamp into a naive exchange-local `datetime`, so its date is the exchange's own trading day. Used by the four price scripts and `app/stock_information/`.
- `fetch_crumb(session)` — Picks up the consent cookie on `session` (trying each of `COOKIE_URLS` until one answers) and returns the crumb from `CRUMB_URL`, or `None` if that request errors or the body isn't a short, non-HTML token. Callers decide whether a missing crumb is fatal (`market_top_volume.py` raises; `stock_intrinsic_value.py` falls back to crumbless requests).
- `crumb_session()` — Returns `(session, crumb)`: a fresh `requests.Session` carrying `HEADERS`, plus `fetch_crumb(session)`.
- `fetch_quote_summary(symbol, modules)` — GETs `quoteSummary` for the given `modules` (with `formatted=false`) through a `crumb_session()`, trying each of `QUOTE_SUMMARY_HOSTS` with the crumb, then each without it. Returns the first `result` — a dict keyed by module, omitting any Yahoo has no data for — or raises `requests.RequestException` with the last error (an error envelope, a non-JSON body's HTTP status, or `no fundamentals in response`).
- `summary_num(node, *path)` / `summary_text(node, *path)` — Dig a value out of a nested `quoteSummary` dict. `summary_num` unwraps Yahoo's `{"raw": …}` and returns a float or `None` (bools and strings are not numbers); `summary_text` returns a non-empty string or `None`.
- `build_params(interval="1d", range_=None, start=None, end=None, last=None)` — Returns the chart-endpoint query params. The window is chosen by, in priority order: an explicit `start`/`end` pair (→ `period1`/`period2`, end padded a day), then `last` (→ a calendar look-back widened for `1wk` / `1mo` bars), then `range_`. Raises `ValueError` for `end` without `start` or an `end` not after `start`. `includeAdjustedClose=true` is always set. Callers pass `interval="1d"` (the default) for daily data.
- `fetch_history(symbol, params)` — Used only by `app/stock_information/` now; the price scripts call `price_source.fetch_history()` instead. GETs the v8 chart endpoint, raises `requests.RequestException` on a Yahoo error body or an empty result, and returns the first `chart.result` object.
- `extract_series(result)` — Returns `(meta, series)` where `series` is a dict of parallel lists straight off the payload — `timestamp`, `open`, `high`, `low`, `close`, `volume` — plus `adjclose` (a list or `None`) and the resolved `gmtoffset`. Callers turn these into row dicts.
- `meta_summary(meta, fallback_symbol=None)` — Pulls the display fields out of a chart result's `meta` block: returns `(symbol, exchange, currency)`, with `symbol` falling back to `fallback_symbol` then `"?"`, `exchange` to `"?"`, and `currency` to `""`. Used by `stock_price_history.py`, `stock_candlestick_chart.py` and `stock_risk.py` for their report headers.

### Notes / limitations

- Changing a constant, the window flags or the `build_params` window logic here affects **all four** price scripts that use it (and `price_source.window()`, which reads `build_params()` output), and changing the crumb handshake or `fetch_quote_summary()` affects `market_top_volume.py` (handshake only), `stock_intrinsic_value.py` (and through it `stock_fundamental.py` and `app/stock_information/`), `stock_earnings_calendar.py`, `stock_analyst_targets.py` and `stock_holders.py`. Behaviour was kept identical to the pre-refactor scripts: the interval-aware `--last` look-back is a no-op for the default `interval="1d"`, so `stock_price_history.py` gets the same params it did before.

---

## price_source.py

Shared price-history source for [`stock_price_history.py`](#stock_price_historypy), [`stock_candlestick_chart.py`](#stock_candlestick_chartpy), [`stock_rebased_chart.py`](#stock_rebased_chartpy) and [`stock_risk.py`](#stock_riskpy), in place of Yahoo Finance's chart endpoint (which fails the [source audit](docs/source-audit.md)). `fetch_history(symbol, params)` takes a ticker in Yahoo notation and the params `yahoo_finance.build_params()` makes, routes the ticker to a keyed provider, and returns the bars in the chart-result shape `yahoo_finance.extract_series()` already parses, so the scripts' row shaping didn't change. Not a CLI: it is imported, not run.

| Yahoo ticker | Provider | Provider symbol |
|---|---|---|
| `AAPL` (no suffix) | EODHD | `AAPL.US` |
| `.L`, `.DE`, `.PA`, `.TO`, `.AX`, `.HK` and the other suffixes in `EODHD_EXCHANGES` | EODHD | `.LSE`, `.XETRA`, `.PA`, `.TO`, `.AU`, `.HK`, … |
| `^GSPC` | EODHD | `GSPC.INDX` |
| `EURUSD=X` / `JPY=X` | EODHD | `EURUSD.FOREX` / `USDJPY.FOREX` |
| `BTC-USD` | EODHD | `BTC-USD.CC` |
| `.NS`, `.BO` (India) | Alpha Vantage | `.BSE` |
| `.SI`, `.T`, `=F` | none | refused with a clear error, for now |

The markets were decided on 2026-10-21 (see the Yahoo item in [`docs/things-to-explore.md`](docs/things-to-explore.md)): neither provider was found to cover Singapore or Tokyo, and futures wait for Databento.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+ and [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `eodhd_price_history.py`, `alphavantage_price_history.py`, `daily_prices.py` and `cli_utils.py` (run from the repo root)
- an `EODHD_API_KEY`, plus an `ALPHAVANTAGE_API_KEY` for Indian stocks, in `.env` or the environment

### Constants

| Constant | Description |
|---|---|
| `EODHD_EXCHANGES` | Yahoo suffix → (EODHD exchange code, exchange name, currency). Codes and names come from EODHD's `exchanges-list`; US, LSE, XETRA, PA, TO, AU and HK were tested on 2026-10-21. LSE and JSE quote in minor units (`GBp`, `ZAc`), as on Yahoo |
| `ALPHAVANTAGE_EXCHANGES` | `.NS` and `.BO` → BSE in INR (Alpha Vantage has BSE listings, not NSE ones) |
| `UNSUPPORTED_EXCHANGES` | `.SI` Singapore and `.T` Tokyo, refused for now |
| `CRYPTO_QUOTES` | Quote currencies that mark `COIN-CCY` as crypto rather than a share class like `BRK-B` |
| `RANGE_DAYS` | Calendar days behind each `--range` keyword (`5d` keeps the last 5 sessions, `ytd` starts on 1 January, `max` asks for everything) |
| `ALPHAVANTAGE_MIN_GAP` | 1.1 seconds between Alpha Vantage calls; free keys refuse a second request within a second |

### API reference

- `route(symbol)`: returns `(provider, provider_symbol, exchange, currency)`, or raises `ValueError` for futures, Singapore, Tokyo or an unknown suffix. Indices get currency `points`; currency pairs and crypto their quote currency.
- `window(params, today=None)`: turns `build_params()` output into inclusive `(first, final, keep)` dates; `keep` is 5 for the `5d` range.
- `aggregate(rows, interval)`: daily rows as `1wk` or `1mo` bars, dated the Monday of the week or the 1st of the month: first open, high / low extremes, last close and adjusted close, summed volume.
- `chart_result(symbol, exchange, currency, source, notes, rows)`: rows in the chart-result shape, with UTC-midnight timestamps and `gmtoffset` 0, plus `meta.source` and `meta.notes`.
- `fetch_daily(provider, provider_symbol, first, final, api_key)`: the provider's daily rows and notes, through `eodhd_price_history.fetch_eod()` or `alphavantage_price_history.fetch_daily()` (spaced by `ALPHAVANTAGE_MIN_GAP`).
- `fetch_history(symbol, params)`: the whole path. Raises `ValueError` for an unsupported ticker, a missing key or an unknown window, and `requests.RequestException` naming the provider (key redacted) when it fails or refuses.

### Notes / limitations

- **Free plans:** EODHD gives one year of history at 20 calls a day; Alpha Vantage the latest 100 sessions at 25 a day, with no adjusted close. A window reaching further back comes back shorter, with a note in `meta.notes` that the scripts print. Every symbol fetched is one call.
- **Indices:** `^GSPC`, `^IXIC` and `^HSI` were confirmed; `^FTSE` (as `FTSE.INDX`) returns no data, so EODHD names it differently, and other indices are untested.
- **No exchange or currency from the providers:** both come from the routing tables, so an LSE listing quoted in USD would still be labelled `GBp`.
- **Dates** are the providers' session dates, with no exchange-local shift.

---

## eodhd_price_history.py

Prints the past daily open / high / low / close prices (plus adjusted close and volume) of a stock, index, currency pair or crypto coin, using [EODHD](https://eodhd.com/)'s end-of-day API (`eodhd.com/api/eod/<symbol>`). It's one of the two keyed price sources chosen to replace Yahoo Finance (see the Yahoo item in [`docs/things-to-explore.md`](docs/things-to-explore.md)). [`price_source.py`](#price_sourcepy) uses its fetch for the four price scripts; run on its own, it takes EODHD's notation.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `daily_prices.py` and `cli_utils.py` modules (no install — run from the repo root so they import)
- an `EODHD_API_KEY` (free registration at [eodhd.com/register](https://eodhd.com/register))

### Usage

```bash
python3 eodhd_price_history.py SYMBOL [--start YYYY-MM-DD] [--end YYYY-MM-DD] [--last N] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SYMBOL` (positional) | — | Ticker in EODHD notation, `SYMBOL.EXCHANGE`: `AAPL.US`, `VOD.LSE`, `SAP.XETRA`, `MC.PA`, `RY.TO`, `BHP.AU`, `0700.HK`; indices (`GSPC.INDX`), currency pairs (`EURUSD.FOREX`), crypto (`BTC-USD.CC`) |
| `--start` / `--end` | — | Explicit date window, inclusive (`--end` defaults to today and needs `--start`) |
| `--last N` | — | Return only the most recent `N` trading sessions (within `--start`/`--end` if given) |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report |

With no window flags, the last 31 calendar days are returned.

#### Example output

```
AAPL.US - EODHD - 3 session(s)

Date                Open        High         Low       Close         Volume
---------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-16      346.0900    347.8200    340.4600    343.4600     59,946,400
2026-10-19      340.7800    346.0000    340.1200    342.9400     34,573,200
2026-10-20      339.6080    343.0600    338.7000    339.5000     35,678,066

Most recent session (2026-10-20): O 339.6080  H 343.0600  L 338.7000  C 339.5000
```

An `Adj Close` column is added when any adjusted close in the window differs from the raw close.

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 eodhd_price_history.py 0700.HK --last 2 --json
```

```json
{
  "symbol": "0700.HK",
  "source": "EODHD",
  "notes": [],
  "prices": [
    { "date": "2026-10-16", "open": 424.0, "high": 428.8, "low": 415.8, "close": 417.8, "adj_close": 417.8, "volume": 18752867 },
    { "date": "2026-10-20", "open": 413.2, "high": 415.6, "low": 406.4, "close": 410.4, "adj_close": 410.4, "volume": 22840013 }
  ]
}
```

`prices` has the same row shape as `stock_price_history.py`. `notes` carries any warning EODHD attached to the data.

### How it works

1. **Resolve the window**: `daily_prices.resolve_window()` turns `--start`/`--end`/`--last` into an inclusive pair of calendar dates. It rejects a bad date before any request, and before the key is even read.
2. **Fetch**: `fetch_eod()` GETs `/api/eod/<symbol>` with `api_token`, `fmt=json`, `period=d`, `from` and `to`. A non-200 answer (an unknown ticker is `404 Ticker Not Found.`) or a body that isn't a JSON list is an error.
3. **Extract**: `extract_rows()` builds the rows with `daily_prices.price_row()`, skips bars without a close, sorts them oldest first, and collects any `warning` field into `notes`.
4. **Trim and display**: rows outside the window are dropped, then `--last N` keeps the final `N`. `daily_prices.print_table()` prints the table, or `--json` prints `{symbol, source, notes, prices}`.

### Configuration

Set `EODHD_API_KEY` as an environment variable or in `.env` at the repo root (a real environment variable wins). Constants at the top of the file:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `EOD_URL` | `https://eodhd.com/api/eod/{symbol}` | End-of-day endpoint |
| `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` | 15 | Request timeout in seconds |
| `SOURCE` | `"EODHD"` | The `source` field and report title |

### API reference

- `fetch_eod(symbol, first, final, api_key)`: returns EODHD's bar list. Raises `ValueError` with EODHD's message for a non-200 answer or a non-list body, and `requests.RequestException` for a network failure.
- `extract_rows(payload)`: returns `(rows, notes)`. Rows are `{date, open, high, low, close, adj_close, volume}`, oldest first; notes are the distinct `warning` values.
- `parse_args(argv=None)` / `main(argv=None)`: the CLI. Errors exit through `cli_utils.die()`, with the API key replaced by `<redacted>` in any message.

### Error handling

- A missing `EODHD_API_KEY`, a failed request, an unknown ticker or an unexpected body exits with code 1: plain text on stderr, or `{"error": "..."}` with `--json`.
- A malformed date, `--end` without `--start`, or an end before the start exits with code 1, before any request.
- A `--last` below 1 is rejected by argument parsing (exit code 2).
- If no sessions fall in the window, it prints `No settled price data found for <SYMBOL>.` (or `"prices": []`) and exits normally.

### Notes / limitations

- **Free plan:** 20 calls a day and one year of history. A request reaching further back gets one recent bar marked `"Data is limited by one year as you have free subscription"`. The script drops that out-of-window bar and reports the warning as a note. The paid "All World" plan ($19.99/month) gives 30+ years.
- **Coverage tested on 2026-10-21:** US, London, XETRA, Paris, Toronto, ASX, Hong Kong, indices, FX and crypto are covered. Singapore (`D05.SG`), India (`RELIANCE.NSE`) and Tokyo (`7203.TSE`) returned 404. The response carries no exchange or currency, so neither is shown.
- **Terms:** for non-professional, personal use. The data may be stored and analyzed but not redistributed or displayed to others (see [`docs/source-audit.md`](docs/source-audit.md)).

---

## alphavantage_price_history.py

Prints the past daily open / high / low / close prices and volume of a stock, using [Alpha Vantage](https://www.alphavantage.co/)'s `TIME_SERIES_DAILY` API. It's the second keyed price source, for the markets EODHD lacks (India). [`price_source.py`](#price_sourcepy) uses its fetch for Indian tickers (`.NS`, `.BO`).

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `daily_prices.py` and `cli_utils.py` modules (run from the repo root)
- an `ALPHAVANTAGE_API_KEY` (free at [alphavantage.co/support/#api-key](https://www.alphavantage.co/support/#api-key))

### Usage

```bash
python3 alphavantage_price_history.py SYMBOL [--start YYYY-MM-DD] [--end YYYY-MM-DD] [--last N] [--full] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SYMBOL` (positional) | — | Ticker in Alpha Vantage notation: `AAPL`, `TSCO.LON`, `MBG.DEX`, `MC.PAR`, `SHOP.TRT`, `RELIANCE.BSE` |
| `--start` / `--end` | — | Explicit date window, inclusive (`--end` defaults to today and needs `--start`) |
| `--last N` | — | Return only the most recent `N` trading sessions |
| `--full` | off | Fetch the whole history (`outputsize=full`) instead of the latest 100 sessions. Premium keys only |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report |

With no window flags, the last 31 calendar days are returned.

#### Example output

```
TSCO.LON - Alpha Vantage - 3 session(s)

Date                Open        High         Low       Close         Volume
---------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-16      530.0000    536.6000    526.4000    535.0000     13,353,793
2026-10-19      534.6000    539.6000    533.4000    539.0000     15,538,387
2026-10-20      543.0000    548.6000    536.4000    539.0000     13,651,054

Most recent session (2026-10-20): O 543.0000  H 548.6000  L 536.4000  C 539.0000
```

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 alphavantage_price_history.py RELIANCE.BSE --start 2025-01-01 --last 2 --json
```

```json
{
  "symbol": "RELIANCE.BSE",
  "source": "Alpha Vantage",
  "notes": [
    "Only the latest 100 sessions are available (from 2026-05-27); the full history needs a premium Alpha Vantage key."
  ],
  "prices": [
    { "date": "2026-10-16", "open": 1214.1, "high": 1215.0, "low": 1192.75, "close": 1210.0, "adj_close": null, "volume": 1021134 },
    { "date": "2026-10-19", "open": 1213.5, "high": 1228.0, "low": 1201.1, "close": 1202.8, "adj_close": null, "volume": 1084569 }
  ]
}
```

### How it works

1. **Resolve the window** with `daily_prices.resolve_window()`, as in `eodhd_price_history.py`.
2. **Fetch**: `fetch_daily()` GETs `www.alphavantage.co/query` with `function=TIME_SERIES_DAILY`, `outputsize=compact` (or `full` with `--full`) and `apikey`. Alpha Vantage refuses with HTTP 200 and a message: `Error Message` for an unknown symbol, `Information` for a premium-only request or the daily limit, or `Note`. Any of these is raised as the error. An empty body (seen under rate limiting) is an error too.
3. **Extract**: `extract_rows()` converts the string values under `"1. open"` … `"5. volume"` into rows, oldest first. `adj_close` is always `null`, because this endpoint has no adjusted close.
4. **Note a short history**: `history_note()` adds a note when a compact answer (100 sessions) starts after the requested window does.
5. **Trim and display**, as in `eodhd_price_history.py`; `--json` prints `{symbol, source, notes, prices}`.

### Configuration

Set `ALPHAVANTAGE_API_KEY` as an environment variable or in `.env` at the repo root. Constants at the top of the file:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `QUERY_URL` | `https://www.alphavantage.co/query` | Alpha Vantage's single query endpoint |
| `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` | 15 | Request timeout in seconds |
| `SOURCE` | `"Alpha Vantage"` | The `source` field and report title |
| `SERIES_KEY` | `"Time Series (Daily)"` | Payload key holding the bars |
| `COMPACT_SESSIONS` | 100 | Sessions in a compact answer |
| `MESSAGE_KEYS` | `("Error Message", "Information", "Note")` | Payload keys that carry a refusal |

### API reference

- `fetch_daily(symbol, api_key, full=False)`: returns the payload. Raises `ValueError` with Alpha Vantage's message on a refusal, an empty body or a missing series, and `requests.RequestException` for a network failure.
- `extract_rows(payload)`: returns the `{date, open, high, low, close, adj_close, volume}` rows, oldest first.
- `history_note(rows, first, full)`: returns the short-history note, or `None`.
- `parse_args(argv=None)` / `main(argv=None)`: the CLI. Errors exit through `cli_utils.die()`, with the key redacted.

### Error handling

- A missing `ALPHAVANTAGE_API_KEY`, a failed request, a refusal (e.g. `Invalid API call…` for an unknown symbol, or the premium message for `--full` on a free key) or an empty body exits with code 1: plain text on stderr, or `{"error": "..."}` with `--json`.
- Date errors exit with code 1 before any request; a `--last` below 1 exits with code 2.
- If no sessions fall in the window, it prints `No settled price data found for <SYMBOL>.` and exits normally.

### Notes / limitations

- **Free key:** 25 requests a day, and only the latest 100 sessions (about five months). `--full` and adjusted closes need premium ($49.99/month and up).
- **Coverage tested on 2026-10-21:** US, London, XETRA, Paris, Toronto and India's BSE (no NSE listing) are covered. Singapore (`D05.SGX`), Tokyo (`7203.TYO`) and Hong Kong (`0700.HKG`) returned "Invalid API call"; ASX was inconclusive.
- **Time zone:** the payload's "Meta Data" time zone is ignored, because it said `US/Eastern` even for `RELIANCE.BSE`. Dates are taken as Alpha Vantage gives them.
- **Terms:** free use is "personal, non-commercial" (see [`docs/source-audit.md`](docs/source-audit.md)).

---

## daily_prices.py

Shared helper module for [`eodhd_price_history.py`](#eodhd_price_historypy) and [`alphavantage_price_history.py`](#alphavantage_price_historypy). It holds the `--start`/`--end`/`--last` flags and their resolution to calendar dates, the `{date, open, high, low, close, adj_close, volume}` row (the same row as `stock_price_history.py`), and the text table both scripts print. Each script keeps its own fetch and payload parsing. It doesn't import `yahoo_finance.py`. Not a CLI: it is imported, not run.

### Constants

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `DEFAULT_DAYS` | 31 | Calendar days looked back when no `--start` or `--last` is given |

### API reference

- `parse_date(value)`: parses `YYYY-MM-DD` to a `date`, raising `ValueError("invalid date ...")`.
- `add_window_args(parser, last_help)`: adds `--start`, `--end` and `--last N` (`cli_utils.positive_int`).
- `resolve_window(start=None, end=None, last=None, today=None)`: returns the inclusive `(first, final)` dates. `start`/`end` (end defaults to today) wins. Otherwise `last` looks back `N*2 + 10` days for the caller to trim. Otherwise `DEFAULT_DAYS`. Raises `ValueError` for a bad date, `end` without `start`, or an end before the start (a single day is allowed).
- `price_row(date, open_, high, low, close, adj_close, volume)`: one row, with prices rounded to 4 dp and volume an int. Accepts numeric strings; missing values become `None`.
- `in_window(rows, first, final)`: the rows dated within the window.
- `print_table(title, rows, currency="")`: prints the title with a session count, the table (with an `Adj Close` column only when some adjusted close differs), and the most recent session's OHLC.

---

## app/stock_information/

A small local web app at `app/stock_information/stock_info_server.py`, separate from the CLI scripts above. It serves a page with a stock-symbol text box and a **Submit** button; on submit it shows four sections: **Company** (name; symbol; sector · industry; market cap · trailing P/E · dividend yield), **Price** (current price, its movement and the previous close; then a day-range bar and a 52-week-range bar, each with a marker at the current price), **Fundamentals** (margins, ROE/ROA, growth, P/E, PEG, EV/EBITDA, P/B, P/S, FCF yield, debt/equity, net debt/EBITDA, current/quick ratio, free and operating cash flow, cash conversion, dividend yield and payout ratio, in six groups), and **Valuations** (how many estimates say the price is undervalued / fair value (within 1%) / overvalued, then a table of the estimates). The quote comes from [`yahoo_finance.py`](#yahoo_financepy) (plus `extract_rows()` from [`stock_price_history.py`](#stock_price_historypy) as a fallback). The fundamental indicators come from [`stock_fundamental.py`](#stock_fundamentalpy)'s `extract_indicators()`, and the valuations from [`stock_intrinsic_value.py`](#stock_intrinsic_valuepy)'s pipeline, run with its default assumptions. See [`app/stock_information/README.md`](app/stock_information/README.md) for the full walkthrough and [`app/stock_information/docs/features.md`](app/stock_information/docs/features.md) for the feature spec.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)

### Usage

```bash
python3 app/stock_information/stock_info_server.py [--host HOST] [--port PORT]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `--host HOST` | `127.0.0.1` | Interface to bind |
| `--port PORT` | `8000` | Port to listen on |

#### Example

```bash
python3 app/stock_information/stock_info_server.py
# Serving Stock Information at http://127.0.0.1:8000/ (Ctrl+C to stop)
```

Open <http://127.0.0.1:8000/>, enter e.g. `AAPL`, and click **Submit**. The page calls the server's `GET /api/quote?symbol=AAPL`, which returns JSON: `{name, symbol, exchange, currency, price, price_time, session_date, session_high, session_low, previous_close, change, change_pct, week52_low, week52_high}`.

### How it works

1. `GET /` serves the inline HTML page.
2. On submit, the page calls `GET /api/quote?symbol=...`.
3. The handler validates the symbol, calls `yahoo_finance.fetch_history()` over a `5d` range, and reads `longName`, `regularMarketPrice`, `regularMarketDayHigh`/`Low`, `regularMarketTime` and `fiftyTwoWeekLow`/`High` from the result's `meta`, falling back to the latest settled daily bar if the price/day fields are missing (the 52-week fields are `null` if absent). The previous close is the close of the last daily bar dated before the latest session, and `change`/`change_pct` are measured against it.
4. The page then calls `GET /api/valuation?symbol=...`, which runs `stock_intrinsic_value.py`'s `fetch_fundamentals()` → `collect_inputs()` → `resolve_assumptions()` → `compute_estimates()` → `build_json()` with its default arguments. It returns the same payload as `stock_intrinsic_value.py SYMBOL --json` plus an `indicators` dict from `stock_fundamental.extract_indicators()` (read from the same quoteSummary modules, with a few derived ratios such as PEG, FCF yield, net debt/EBITDA and cash conversion) and a `summary` (`{undervalued, fair_value, overvalued, not_available, threshold}`) from the server's `summarize_estimates()`, which counts the seven per-share estimates against the price: an estimate above the price counts as undervalued, below as overvalued, and less than 1% away as fair value. The page fills in sector/industry from the payload's top-level fields and market cap, trailing P/E and dividend yield (`dividend_rate / price`) from its `inputs`, renders `indicators` as a grouped Fundamentals table, shows the counts as three tiles, and renders the estimates as a table (value per share and price-vs-estimate for each method, reverse-DCF implied growth, and the assumptions used).

A server is used instead of a static page because Yahoo's endpoints don't send CORS headers, so a browser can't call them directly.

### Error handling

- Invalid symbol → HTTP 400; Yahoo error / unknown ticker / network failure → HTTP 502. Either way the page shows `Lookup failed: <message>`.
- A failed `/api/valuation` call (HTTP 502) leaves the quote on screen, shows `Fundamentals unavailable: <message>` and `Valuation failed: <message>` in those sections, and marks sector, industry, market cap, P/E and dividend yield as "unavailable".
- Bad `--port` is rejected by argparse (exit code 2).

### Notes / limitations

- Unofficial Yahoo endpoint; Yahoo-notation tickers only (no company-name lookup); price is the last regular-market price (often delayed, no pre/post-market).
- Valuations are mechanical, not forecasts. With the default assumptions, the P/E, EV/EBITDA and P/S rows use the stock's own current multiple and so land at about the price; the page labels them with the multiple used, and they usually count as "fair value" in the tiles. Those counts are a tally of mechanical estimates, not a judgment that the stock is actually under- or overvalued and not a buy/sell signal. The assumptions can't be overridden from the page. Use `stock_intrinsic_value.py`'s flags for that.
- Fundamental indicators share [`stock_fundamental.py`](#stock_fundamentalpy)'s limitations: trailing-12-month figures as reported, quarterly YoY growth, and no interest coverage, ROIC or buybacks/dilution.
- Binds to localhost by default; `--host 0.0.0.0` exposes it with no authentication.

---

# Valuation & Fundamental Analysis

---

## indicators.py

Small, dependency-free technical indicators and risk metrics over a price series — a plain list of numbers, oldest session first, such as the `close` field of each row from [`stock_price_history.py`](#stock_price_historypy)'s `--json` output. Not a CLI: it is imported, not run. Used by [`stock_candlestick_chart.py`](#stock_candlestick_chartpy) (moving-average overlays and cross-flip arrows), [`stock_rebased_chart.py`](#stock_rebased_chartpy) (`rebase`), [`stock_risk.py`](#stock_riskpy) (`volatility`, `max_drawdown`, `correlation`), and the [`stock-trend`](#claude-code-agent-stock-trend) agent. For a concepts-first walkthrough see [`docs/indicators.md`](docs/indicators.md).

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+ (standard library only)

### API reference

- `moving_average(prices, n)` — Simple moving average over an `n`-session window. Returns a list the **same length** as `prices`: element `i` is the mean of `prices[i-n+1 .. i]`, or `None` for the first `n-1` positions where a full window isn't available yet, so the result lines up session-for-session with the source rows. Raises `ValueError` if `n < 1`.
- `price_vs_moving_average(prices, n)` — Where each session's price sits relative to its `n`-session SMA. Returns a list the **same length** as `prices`: `"above"`, `"below"`, or `"equal"` for each session where the SMA is defined, and `None` for the first `n-1` positions where it isn't yet. Take `[-1]` for the current reading. Raises `ValueError` (via `moving_average`) if `n < 1`.
- `moving_average_cross(prices, fast, slow)` — Where the `fast`-session SMA sits relative to the `slow`-session SMA. Returns a list the **same length** as `prices`: `"above"`, `"below"`, or `"equal"` for each session where **both** averages are defined (from index `max(fast, slow) - 1` on), else `None`. Take `[-1]` for the current reading; a change in the value is a crossover (the golden / death cross for e.g. `fast=50, slow=200`). Raises `ValueError` (via `moving_average`) if either window `< 1`.
- `moving_average_cross_flip(prices, fast, slow)` — Marks the sessions where `moving_average_cross` flips sides. Returns a list the **same length** as `prices`: `True` on a session where the cross has just gone `"below"` → `"above"` or `"above"` → `"below"` since the last decisive session, `False` otherwise. Runs of `"equal"` between the two sides are stepped over, so the flip still reports on the session where the new side first shows; `"equal"` sessions, unchanged sessions, and the first decisive session are `False`, and undefined sessions are `None`. Take `[-1]` for the current reading. Raises `ValueError` (via `moving_average`) if either window `< 1`.
- `rebase(prices, n=0)` — Rescales the series so its `n`-th session (default `0`, the first) reads as 100. Returns a list the **same length** as `prices`: element `i` is `prices[i] / prices[n] * 100`, so values above 100 mean the series has risen relative to session `n`, below means it has fallen. Raises `IndexError` if `n` is out of range, `ValueError` if `prices[n]` is `0`.
- `trend(prices, window=None, flat_threshold=0.01)` — Classifies the series as `"up"`, `"down"`, or `"flat"`. Fits a least-squares line through the closes and measures the move that line implies from its first fitted point to its last, as a fraction of the *magnitude* of the mean price (so the sign always tracks the slope, even for an all-negative series); if that fitted move is smaller than `flat_threshold` (default `0.01` = 1%) in magnitude the series is `"flat"` (direction is just noise), otherwise its sign decides up vs down. `window=N` restricts the fit to the last `N` prices. Raises `ValueError` on fewer than 2 prices (or `window < 2`).
- `returns(prices)` — Session-to-session fractional change. Returns a list the **same length** as `prices`: element `i` is `prices[i] / prices[i-1] - 1` (`0.05` = up 5%), `None` at index 0 (an empty input gives `[]`). Raises `ValueError` if any price but the last is `0`.
- `volatility(prices, periods_per_year=252)` — Annualized volatility: the sample standard deviation (`n - 1` denominator) of `returns(prices)`, times `sqrt(periods_per_year)`. Returns a fraction (`0.24` = 24% a year); pass `periods_per_year=1` for the per-session figure, `52` for weekly bars, `365` for a 7-day-a-week crypto series. Up and down moves count alike. Takes the whole series — slice it first (`prices[-21:]`) for a recent stretch. Raises `ValueError` on fewer than 3 prices, `periods_per_year <= 0`, or a zero price.
- `max_drawdown(prices)` — The worst peak-to-trough fall against the running high. Returns `{"drawdown", "peak", "trough", "recovery"}`: `drawdown` is a fraction `<= 0` (`-0.25` = down 25% from the peak), `peak` / `trough` are the indices of the high and the low, and `recovery` is the index of the first later session that closed at or above the peak's price (`None` if none has). On a tie the earliest fall wins. With no fall at all, `drawdown` is `0.0` and the three indices are `None`. Raises `ValueError` on an empty series or a non-positive price.
- `correlation(prices_a, prices_b)` — Pearson correlation, from `-1` to `+1`, of the two series' **returns** (not raw prices, which would make any two up-trending series look related). The lists must be the same length and aligned session for session; matching up dates across exchanges with different holidays is the caller's job. Returns `None` if either series' returns never vary (a flat or constant-growth series). Raises `ValueError` on a length mismatch, fewer than 3 prices, or a zero price.
- `_ols_slope(values)` — Internal: least-squares slope of `values` against `x = 0, 1, 2, …` (per step).

### Examples

```python
>>> from indicators import (
...     moving_average, price_vs_moving_average, moving_average_cross,
...     moving_average_cross_flip, rebase, trend, returns, volatility,
...     max_drawdown, correlation)
>>> moving_average([1, 2, 3, 4, 5], 3)
[None, None, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0]
>>> price_vs_moving_average([10, 10, 10, 7, 13], 3)
[None, None, 'equal', 'below', 'above']
>>> moving_average_cross([1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6], 2, 4)
[None, None, None, 'above', 'above', 'above']
>>> moving_average_cross_flip([3, 1, 1, 2, 3], 1, 2)
[None, False, False, True, False]
>>> rebase([50, 100, 150])
[100.0, 200.0, 300.0]
>>> rebase([50, 100, 150], n=1)
[50.0, 100.0, 150.0]
>>> trend([1, 2, 3, 4, 5])
'up'
>>> trend([5, 4, 3, 2, 1])
'down'
>>> trend([10, 10, 10, 10])
'flat'
>>> returns([100, 150, 75])
[None, 0.5, -0.5]
>>> round(volatility([100, 150, 75], periods_per_year=1), 4)
0.7071
>>> max_drawdown([100, 120, 90, 110])
{'drawdown': -0.25, 'peak': 1, 'trough': 2, 'recovery': None}
>>> correlation([100, 150, 75], [10, 15, 7.5])
1.0
```

Pull real closes straight from the JSON tool:

```python
import json, subprocess
from indicators import (
    trend, moving_average, price_vs_moving_average, moving_average_cross,
    moving_average_cross_flip, rebase, volatility, max_drawdown)

out = subprocess.run(
    ["python3", "stock_price_history.py", "AAPL", "--range", "6mo", "--json"],
    capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,
).stdout
closes = [r["close"] for r in json.loads(out)["prices"]]

trend(closes)                 # -> "up" / "down" / "flat" over the whole span
trend(closes, window=20)      # -> the near-term (last 20 sessions) trend
moving_average(closes, 50)[-1]                 # -> current 50-session SMA
price_vs_moving_average(closes, 50)[-1]        # -> "above" / "below" / "equal"
moving_average_cross(closes, 20, 50)[-1]       # -> is the 20-SMA above the 50-SMA?
moving_average_cross_flip(closes, 20, 50)[-1]  # -> did the 20/50 cross just flip sides?
rebase(closes)[-1]            # -> current price as a ratio of the first session, x100
volatility(closes)            # -> annualized volatility over the span, e.g. 0.24
volatility(closes[-21:])      # -> ... over roughly the last month
max_drawdown(closes)          # -> worst fall: {"drawdown": -0.12, "peak": 41, ...}
```

### Notes / limitations

- `moving_average` re-sums each window, so it is O(len·n) — fine for stock series (hundreds–thousands of points). For very long inputs keep a running sum instead (O(len)), accepting minor floating-point drift. `price_vs_moving_average`, `moving_average_cross`, and `moving_average_cross_flip` build on it and inherit that cost.
- `trend` is a mechanical read of past closing prices — a straight-line fit, nothing more. It is not a forecast, and `flat_threshold` is a blunt single knob: tune it up to ignore weaker trends, down to be more sensitive. It does not scale with the series' own volatility.
- The risk metrics (`volatility`, `max_drawdown`, `correlation`) describe the past period only — past swings and falls are no promise of future ones — and all three move a lot with the window chosen. `volatility` is symmetric (a big jump up scores like a big drop) and assumes the bars are evenly spaced at `periods_per_year`. `correlation` says whether two series moved in step, not by how much, nor why.
- All of these take raw `close` values. Pass `adj_close` instead when spanning a split or large dividend — a split shows up in raw closes as a huge one-day "fall", which would wreck `returns`, `volatility` and `max_drawdown` in particular.

---

## valuation.py

Small, dependency-free intrinsic-value estimators for a single stock — the arithmetic of the common methods (discounted cash flow, valuation multiples, reverse DCF) made explicit and composable. Intrinsic value is never one number: every function is just a formula over the assumptions you pass it. Rates are fractions, not percents (`0.09` == 9%); cash-flow and value inputs are in whatever currency unit you supply, and per-share outputs are that unit divided by the share count. Not a CLI: it is imported, not run. For a concepts-first walkthrough see [`docs/valuations.md`](docs/valuations.md).

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+ (standard library only)

### API reference

- `gordon_growth_value(cash_flow, discount_rate, growth)` — Present value of a cash flow growing forever at a constant rate: `cash_flow / (discount_rate - growth)`. `cash_flow` is the amount expected **one year out** (D1). One formula, three uses: a perpetuity, a constant-growth dividend discount model, and the terminal value inside a DCF. Raises `ValueError` unless `discount_rate > growth`.
- `discounted_cash_flow(fcf, discount_rate, growth, years=None, terminal_growth=0.02, net_debt=0.0, shares=None)` — Two-stage DCF. Grows `fcf` (the latest actual free cash flow) for an explicit forecast — `growth` as one rate over `years`, or an iterable of per-year rates — discounts each year at `discount_rate`, adds a `gordon_growth_value` terminal value discounted from the horizon, then nets `net_debt` and divides by `shares` (via `equity_from_enterprise`). Returns total equity value, or per-share value when `shares` is given. Raises `ValueError` on a missing/`< 1` horizon, an empty growth list, or `terminal_growth >= discount_rate`.
- `multiple_value(metric_per_share, multiple)` — Per-share value from an **equity** multiple: `metric_per_share * multiple`. Pass EPS for P/E, sales/share for P/S, book value/share for P/B, FCF/share for P/FCF.
- `ev_multiple_value(metric, multiple, net_debt=0.0, shares=None)` — Value from an **enterprise** multiple (EV/EBITDA, EV/Sales): `metric * multiple` is the implied enterprise value, then `equity_from_enterprise` nets debt and divides by shares. `metric` is the whole-company figure (total EBITDA, total sales).
- `equity_from_enterprise(enterprise_value, net_debt=0.0, shares=None)` — Enterprise value → equity value: subtract `net_debt` (a negative value, i.e. net cash, is added back), then divide by `shares` if given. Raises `ValueError` if `shares <= 0`.
- `implied_growth_rate(price, fcf, discount_rate, years, terminal_growth=0.02, net_debt=0.0, shares=None, tol=1e-9, max_iter=200)` — Reverse DCF: bisects for the constant forecast-stage growth rate at which `discounted_cash_flow(...)` equals `price` (per share if `shares` is given, else a total equity value). Raises `ValueError` if the price can't be bracketed for growth in `(-0.999, 20]`.
- `_growth_path(growth, years)` — Internal: normalises `growth` to a list of per-year rates (a single rate repeated `years` times, or an iterable used as-is).

### Examples

```python
>>> from valuation import (
...     gordon_growth_value, discounted_cash_flow, multiple_value,
...     ev_multiple_value, equity_from_enterprise, implied_growth_rate)
>>> gordon_growth_value(5.0, 0.08, 0.03)          # D1=5, r=8%, g=3%
100.0
>>> round(discounted_cash_flow(100, 0.09, 0.05, 5), 2)   # FCF 100, WACC 9%, 5% for 5y
1656.27
>>> round(discounted_cash_flow(100, 0.09, [0.08, 0.06, 0.04], shares=100), 4)
16.2701
>>> multiple_value(6.5, 28)                       # EPS 6.5 at a 28x P/E
182.0
>>> round(ev_multiple_value(120_000, 14, net_debt=200_000, shares=15_000), 4)
98.6667
>>> round(implied_growth_rate(1656.27, 100, 0.09, 5), 4)  # reverse the DCF above
0.05
```

Feed it real free cash flow from an earnings source and pair it with the price tools here:

```python
import json, subprocess
from valuation import discounted_cash_flow, implied_growth_rate

# From a 10-K / earnings feed (not fetched by this repo):
fcf = 100_000_000_000        # trailing free cash flow
shares = 15_000_000_000
net_debt = -50_000_000_000   # net cash

fair = discounted_cash_flow(fcf, 0.09, 0.06, 10,
                            terminal_growth=0.025,
                            net_debt=net_debt, shares=shares)

out = subprocess.run(
    ["python3", "stock_price_history.py", "AAPL", "--last", "1", "--json"],
    capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,
).stdout
price = json.loads(out)["prices"][-1]["close"]

fair                                  # -> per-share DCF value under these assumptions
price / fair - 1                      # -> premium (+) / discount (-) to that value
implied_growth_rate(price, fcf, 0.09, 10,
                    terminal_growth=0.025,
                    net_debt=net_debt, shares=shares)  # -> growth the price bakes in
```

### Notes / limitations

- Garbage in, garbage out. A DCF value swings by large multiples on plausible-looking changes to `discount_rate`, `growth`, and `terminal_growth`; treat the output as one point in a range, not a target. Run a few assumption sets and compare.
- `terminal_growth` must stay below `discount_rate` (and realistically at or below long-run GDP growth). The terminal value is usually the majority of a DCF result, so it dominates the answer.
- `multiple_value` / `ev_multiple_value` only move the judgement to "what multiple is fair" — they don't remove it. Keep equity multiples (P/E, P/S) and enterprise multiples (EV/EBITDA) with the right metric; mixing them is the common mistake.
- No taxes, buybacks, dilution, leases, or mid-year discounting are modelled. `fcf` is whatever you define it as — be consistent (levered FCF for an equity discount rate, unlevered FCF for a WACC).
- These are pure functions with no data source. Feed them fundamentals yourself, or use [`stock_intrinsic_value.py`](#stock_intrinsic_valuepy), which pulls the fundamentals from Yahoo Finance and runs every one of them.

---

## stock_intrinsic_value.py

Pulls the fundamentals a stock's intrinsic value needs from Yahoo Finance's public `quoteSummary` endpoint (`query{1,2}.finance.yahoo.com/v10/finance/quoteSummary/<symbol>`), then runs **every** method in [`valuation.py`](#valuationpy) it has inputs for and prints the estimates side by side against the current price. No API key or authentication required — a consent cookie and crumb are fetched automatically.

What it retrieves per ticker: trailing free cash flow, EBITDA, revenue, share count, total debt and cash, trailing/forward EPS, book value per share, beta, the forward dividend per share (Yahoo's forward dividend yield × price, so it is in the price's own unit — pence for `GBp` listings), the current P/E, P/S and EV/EBITDA multiples, enterprise value, and the analyst growth estimate (`+5y`, falling back to `+1y`).

What it computes:

| Row | `valuation.py` call | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Two-stage DCF | `discounted_cash_flow` | on trailing FCF, per share after netting debt |
| Reverse DCF → implied growth | `implied_growth_rate` | the forecast growth the current price implies |
| Dividend discount | `gordon_growth_value` | grown at the terminal rate; skipped for non-payers |
| P/E multiple | `multiple_value` | on trailing EPS |
| Graham `EPS × (8.5 + 2g)` | `multiple_value` | multiple capped at 40 |
| EV/EBITDA multiple | `ev_multiple_value` | on trailing EBITDA |
| P/S multiple | `multiple_value` | on revenue per share |
| `[check]` reported EV → equity | `equity_from_enterprise` | a consistency check — should ≈ price |

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `yahoo_finance.py` (for its `quoteSummary` fetch and cookie + crumb handshake), `valuation.py`, and `cli_utils.py` — run from the repo root so they import

### Usage

```bash
python3 stock_intrinsic_value.py SYMBOL [--discount-rate R] [--growth G] [--terminal-growth G] [--years N] [--risk-free R] [--equity-risk-premium P] [--pe X] [--ev-ebitda X] [--ps X] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SYMBOL` (positional) | — | Ticker in Yahoo notation: `AAPL`, `VOD.L`, `D05.SI`, `SAP.DE`, … |
| `--discount-rate R` | CAPM from the stock's beta, else `0.09` | Annual discount rate / WACC as a fraction (`0.09` = 9%) |
| `--growth G` | analyst 5-year EPS growth, else 1-year, else `0.05` | Forecast-stage annual FCF growth, as a fraction |
| `--terminal-growth G` | `0.025` | Perpetual growth past the forecast (also the dividend-model growth) |
| `--years N` | `10` | Explicit DCF forecast horizon |
| `--risk-free R` | `0.04` | Risk-free rate for the CAPM discount rate |
| `--equity-risk-premium P` | `0.05` | Equity risk premium for the CAPM discount rate |
| `--pe X` | the stock's current trailing P/E | Fair P/E for the multiple valuation |
| `--ev-ebitda X` | the stock's current EV/EBITDA | Fair EV/EBITDA for the multiple valuation |
| `--ps X` | the stock's current trailing P/S | Fair P/S for the multiple valuation |
| `--json` | off | Emit structured JSON on stdout instead of the report |

The three `--pe` / `--ev-ebitda` / `--ps` multiples **default to the stock's own current multiple**, so those rows reproduce the market's valuation until you pass a considered number. Any input a method needs that Yahoo doesn't return (e.g. free cash flow for a loss-making company) leaves that row blank rather than failing.

#### Example

```bash
python3 stock_intrinsic_value.py AAPL --growth 0.08 --discount-rate 0.09
```

```
AAPL - Apple Inc.  (NasdaqGS - USD)
Price 324.96 USD    Market cap 4.74T    Sector: Technology
...
Assumptions
  Discount rate       9.00%   (user-supplied)
  FCF growth          8.00%   (user-supplied)
  Terminal growth     2.50%
  Forecast years         10

Intrinsic value estimates (per share unless noted)
  Method                                     Value   Price vs est.
  Two-stage DCF                             174.83          +85.9%
  P/E multiple (37.31x)                     324.96           +0.0%
  Graham EPS x (8.5+2g) (24.50x)            213.40          +52.3%
  EV/EBITDA multiple (28.37x)               324.96           -0.0%
  P/S multiple (10.16x)                     324.96           -0.0%
  Dividend discount (g=2.50%)                17.03        +1808.1%
  Reverse DCF -> implied growth             16.16%             (vs assumed 8.00%)
  [check] reported EV -> equity             324.96           -0.0%
  (dividend yield < 1% - the Gordon dividend model is not meaningful for this stock)
```

(Figures move with live data; the P/E, EV/EBITDA and P/S rows equal the price here because their multiples default to the stock's current ones.)

`Price vs est.` is `price / estimate - 1`: positive means the market price sits **above** that method's value.

### How it works

1. **Cookie + crumb** — [`yahoo_finance.crumb_session()`](#yahoo_financepy) opens a `requests.Session` that GETs `fc.yahoo.com` (then `finance.yahoo.com`) to pick up the EU-consent cookie, then `query1.finance.yahoo.com/v1/test/getcrumb` for the crumb token the fundamentals endpoint now requires.
2. **Fetch** — [`yahoo_finance.fetch_quote_summary()`](#yahoo_financepy) GETs `quoteSummary` for `price,summaryDetail,defaultKeyStatistics,financialData,earningsTrend,summaryProfile`, trying `query2` then `query1`, with the crumb and then without. A bad ticker returns an error envelope (raised) or an empty `result` (raised).
3. **Parse** — `collect_inputs()` digs each figure out of the nested modules, unwrapping Yahoo's `{"raw": …}` wrappers, and derives net debt (`totalDebt − totalCash`) and revenue per share.
4. **Assumptions** — `resolve_assumptions()`: the discount rate is the CLI value, else CAPM (`risk_free + beta × equity_risk_premium`), else `0.09`; the growth is the CLI value, else the analyst `+5y` estimate, else `+1y`, else `0.05`. If `terminal_growth ≥ discount_rate` the discount rate is nudged up so the model stays defined. Every choice is shown with its source.
5. **Estimate** — `compute_estimates()` calls each `valuation.py` function whose inputs are present and positive; the rest come back `None` and render blank.
6. **Report** — a human-readable block (fundamentals, assumptions, estimate table) or, with `--json`, `{symbol, name, exchange, currency, sector, industry, price, inputs, assumptions, multiples_used, estimates}`.

### Error handling

- Network failure, a bad ticker, or a response with no usable fundamentals → exits non-zero, printing `Error: …` to stderr (or `{"error": "…"}` with `--json`).
- A `--years` value below 1 is rejected by argument parsing (exit code 2), before any request is made.

### Notes / limitations

- Same caveat as [`valuation.py`](#valuationpy): the output is only as good as the assumptions. The defaults (CAPM discount rate, analyst near-term growth as a 10-year rate, current multiples as "fair") are a **starting point**, not a house view — vary `--discount-rate`, `--growth`, `--terminal-growth` and compare.
- Yahoo's `freeCashflow` is a levered TTM figure; `earningsTrend` growth is an *EPS* estimate used here as an *FCF* growth proxy. Analyst `+5y` is often missing, in which case the `+1y` number stands in and is a poor perpetual-growth guess.
- One trailing snapshot per field — no multi-year averaging, no normalisation for one-off items, no segment detail. `quoteSummary` is an undocumented endpoint and its crumb/cookie flow can change without notice.
- Figures come back in the listing currency; the script does not convert.
- `fetch_fundamentals()` and `collect_inputs()` are also imported by [`stock_fundamental.py`](#stock_fundamentalpy) and [`app/stock_information/`](#appstock_information), so changing them affects both.

---

## Claude Code agent: `stock-intrinsic-value`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/stock-intrinsic-value.md`. Like the other agents here, it calls no API itself — it shells out via the `Bash` tool to [`stock_intrinsic_value.py`](#stock_intrinsic_valuepy), which fetches the fundamentals from Yahoo Finance and runs every method in [`valuation.py`](#valuationpy).

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer requests like "what's AAPL worth?", "is Tesla overvalued?", "run a DCF on Microsoft", "fair value of Novo Nordisk with a 10% discount rate", or "what growth is priced into NVDA?" with model output built from live fundamentals, instead of guessing a number from stale training data.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for questions about a named company's or ticker's intrinsic / fair value, whether it is over- or under-valued, or a DCF / reverse-DCF / multiples valuation, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter. Questions about analysts' price targets go to [`stock-analyst-targets`](#claude-code-agent-stock-analyst-targets) instead.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "stock-intrinsic-value"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Resolves the company to a Yahoo ticker, adding the exchange suffix for non-US listings (`.L`, `.SI`, `.DE`, …); if the ticker is uncertain it says so rather than guessing.
2. Runs `python3 stock_intrinsic_value.py SYMBOL --json` from the repo root with **no assumption flags by default** (the tool derives the discount rate from CAPM, the growth from the analyst estimate). It adds `--discount-rate` / `--growth` / `--terminal-growth` / `--years` / `--pe` / `--ev-ebitda` / `--ps` only when the user states an assumption, and re-runs the tool 2–3 times with bear / base / bull assumption sets when the user wants a considered over/under-valued call.
3. Parses the JSON — `price`, `inputs`, `assumptions` (with the source of each), `multiples_used`, and `estimates` (`dcf_two_stage`, `reverse_dcf_implied_growth`, `dividend_discount`, `pe_multiple`, `graham`, `ev_ebitda_multiple`, `ps_multiple`, `ev_reported_to_equity`), where each estimate's `price_vs_estimate` is `price / value - 1` (positive → market above that estimate).
4. Reports a synthesis: the DCF value (or range across scenarios) versus the price with its key assumptions, then the reverse-DCF implied growth versus what analysts expect, then the other *independent* estimates (a user-set multiple, or the dividend model for real payers), calling out any `null` rows and the missing input behind them. Closes with a caveat that these are assumption-sensitive model outputs from one trailing snapshot, not investment advice. On failure it surfaces the `{"error": "..."}` from stderr and retries at most once.

### Configuration

The agent's frontmatter restricts it to `Bash` (run the script and read its JSON).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`stock_intrinsic_value.py`](#stock_intrinsic_valuepy) |
| Underlying module | [`valuation.py`](#valuationpy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Requires `stock_intrinsic_value.py` (with its `yahoo_finance.py` helper and the `requests` dependency) and `valuation.py` to be present and runnable from the repo root.
- Only as good as the ticker it picks and the assumptions it runs — a DCF swings by large multiples on plausible changes to the discount rate and growth, which is why the agent prefers a scenario range over a single number.
- The multiple-based rows are not independent estimates unless the user supplies the multiple; by default they reproduce the stock's current valuation. The dividend model is meaningless for low- or non-payers, and the `[check]` row is a data-quality signal, not a valuation.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`stock_intrinsic_value.py`](#stock_intrinsic_valuepy) and [`valuation.py`](#valuationpy) (undocumented Yahoo `quoteSummary` endpoint, one trailing snapshot per field, EPS-growth estimate used as an FCF-growth proxy, no normalisation for one-off items, listing-currency figures).

---

## stock_fundamental.py

Pulls a stock's fundamental indicators from Yahoo Finance's public `quoteSummary` endpoint and reports them in six groups. No API key or authentication required. The fetch and parsing are [`stock_intrinsic_value.py`](#stock_intrinsic_valuepy)'s own `fetch_fundamentals()` and `collect_inputs()`, imported rather than copied, so the two scripts read the same data. Its `extract_indicators()` also supplies the **Fundamentals** section of [`app/stock_information/`](#appstock_information).

| Group | Indicators | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Profitability | gross, operating and net margin; return on equity; return on assets | `financialData.grossMargins`, `operatingMargins`, `profitMargins`, `returnOnEquity`, `returnOnAssets` |
| Growth | revenue and earnings growth (YoY, latest quarter); analyst 5-year growth estimate | `financialData.revenueGrowth`, `earningsGrowth`; `earningsTrend` `+5y` |
| Valuation | trailing and forward P/E; PEG; EV/EBITDA; P/B; P/S; free-cash-flow yield | `summaryDetail`, `defaultKeyStatistics`; PEG = trailing P/E ÷ (5y growth × 100), FCF yield = FCF ÷ market cap |
| Financial health | debt/equity; net debt/EBITDA; current ratio; quick ratio | `financialData.debtToEquity` ÷ 100 (Yahoo reports a percentage); (total debt − total cash) ÷ EBITDA; `currentRatio`, `quickRatio` |
| Cash flow | free cash flow; operating cash flow; cash conversion | `financialData.freeCashflow`, `operatingCashflow`; FCF ÷ `defaultKeyStatistics.netIncomeToCommon` |
| Shareholder returns | dividend yield; payout ratio | `summaryDetail.dividendYield` (Yahoo's forward yield, carried as `dividend_rate` = yield × price, then ÷ price); `summaryDetail.payoutRatio` |

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `stock_intrinsic_value.py` (for the fetch and parsing; it in turn needs `yahoo_finance.py` and `valuation.py`) and `cli_utils.py` — run from the repo root so they import

### Usage

```bash
python3 stock_fundamental.py SYMBOL [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SYMBOL` (positional) | — | Ticker in Yahoo notation: `AAPL`, `VOD.L`, `D05.SI`, `SAP.DE`, … |
| `--json` | off | Emit structured JSON on stdout instead of the report |

#### Example

```bash
python3 stock_fundamental.py AAPL
```

```
AAPL - Apple Inc.  (NasdaqGS - USD)
Price 330.32 USD    Market cap 4.82T    Sector: Technology    Industry: Consumer Electronics

Profitability
  Gross margin                                 48.65%
  Operating margin                             32.62%
  Net margin                                   27.62%
  Return on equity (ROE)                      148.75%
  Return on assets (ROA)                       27.08%

Growth
  Revenue growth (YoY)                         16.40%
  Earnings growth (YoY)                        28.70%
  Analyst 5-year growth estimate                    -
...
Shareholder returns
  Dividend yield                                0.33%
  Payout ratio                                 12.04%
```

(Figures move with live data.) With `--json`: `{symbol, name, exchange, currency, sector, industry, price, market_cap, indicators}`, where `indicators` holds the 24 keys (`gross_margin`, `operating_margin`, `net_margin`, `return_on_equity`, `return_on_assets`, `revenue_growth`, `earnings_growth`, `analyst_growth_5y`, `trailing_pe`, `forward_pe`, `peg`, `ev_to_ebitda`, `price_to_book`, `price_to_sales`, `fcf_yield`, `debt_to_equity`, `net_debt_to_ebitda`, `current_ratio`, `quick_ratio`, `free_cash_flow`, `operating_cash_flow`, `cash_conversion`, `dividend_yield`, `payout_ratio`). Margins, returns, growth and yields are fractions (`0.25` = 25%); a missing indicator is `null`.

### How it works

1. **Fetch** — `stock_intrinsic_value.fetch_fundamentals()`: the cookie + crumb handshake, then `quoteSummary` for `price,summaryDetail,defaultKeyStatistics,financialData,earningsTrend,summaryProfile`.
2. **Parse** — `stock_intrinsic_value.collect_inputs()` flattens the figures both scripts share (price, market cap, FCF, EBITDA, net debt, multiples, dividend, analyst growth, sector, industry).
3. **Indicators** — `extract_indicators(modules, inputs)` reuses those and reads the rest directly from the modules, then derives PEG, FCF yield, net debt/EBITDA, cash conversion and dividend yield. A ratio whose denominator is missing or non-positive is `None`, as are PEG with negative growth and a gross margin of exactly 0 (Yahoo's placeholder for banks, which have no cost of goods sold).
4. **Report** — one section per group in `INDICATOR_GROUPS` order, or the JSON object.

### API reference

- `INDICATOR_GROUPS` — `[(group, [(key, label, format), ...]), ...]` in display order; `format` is `"pct"`, `"ratio"` or `"big"`.
- `extract_indicators(modules, inputs)` — Returns the indicators dict from quoteSummary `modules` and `stock_intrinsic_value.collect_inputs(modules)`.
- `build_report(symbol, inputs, indicators)` / `build_json(symbol, inputs, indicators)` — The text report and the JSON payload.
- `parse_args(argv=None)`, `main(argv=None)` — The CLI.

### Error handling

- Network failure, a bad ticker, or a response with no usable fundamentals → exits 1, printing `Error: …` to stderr (or `{"error": "…"}` with `--json`).
- A missing symbol or unknown flag is rejected by argument parsing (exit code 2), before any request is made.

### Notes / limitations

- Trailing-12-month figures as Yahoo reports them — one snapshot, no multi-year history, no adjustment for one-off items. Growth is year over year for the latest quarter, so one unusual quarter can distort it.
- Interest coverage, ROIC and buybacks/dilution aren't reported: the quoteSummary modules fetched don't carry them.
- The analyst 5-year growth estimate is often missing, so PEG is often blank.
- Industry context matters: banks and insurers have no meaningful gross margin, current ratio or debt/equity, and Yahoo often omits them.
- Same undocumented endpoint and fragile crumb/cookie flow as `stock_intrinsic_value.py`. Amounts are in the listing currency.

---

## Claude Code agent: `stock-fundamental`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/stock-fundamental.md`. Like the other agents here, it calls no API itself — it shells out via the `Bash` tool to [`stock_fundamental.py`](#stock_fundamentalpy).

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer requests like "what are Apple's fundamentals?", "what's Tesla's profit margin?", "how much debt does DBS have?", "P/E and PEG for Microsoft" or "is Vodafone's dividend covered?" from live Yahoo Finance figures, instead of stale training data.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for questions about a named company's margins, returns, growth, valuation multiples, debt or liquidity, cash flow or dividend, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter. Intrinsic/fair-value questions go to [`stock-intrinsic-value`](#claude-code-agent-stock-intrinsic-value), earnings and dividend dates to [`stock-earnings-calendar`](#claude-code-agent-stock-earnings-calendar), analyst price targets to [`stock-analyst-targets`](#claude-code-agent-stock-analyst-targets), and shareholders and insider trading to [`stock-holders`](#claude-code-agent-stock-holders) instead.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "stock-fundamental"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Resolves the company to a Yahoo ticker, adding the exchange suffix for non-US listings; if the ticker is uncertain it says so rather than guessing. For comparisons it runs once per ticker.
2. Runs `python3 stock_fundamental.py SYMBOL --json` from the repo root.
3. Parses the JSON (`name`, `sector`, `industry`, `price`, `market_cap`, `indicators`).
4. Answers any specific indicator the user asked about first; otherwise gives a short read of each group and a table of all indicators. It interprets figures against the company's industry, flags ones that can mislead, and names any `null` indicators. Closes with a caveat that these are trailing, descriptive figures from one snapshot, not investment advice. On failure it surfaces the `{"error": "..."}` from stderr and retries at most once.

### Configuration

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`stock_fundamental.py`](#stock_fundamentalpy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Requires `stock_fundamental.py` and its `stock_intrinsic_value.py` / `yahoo_finance.py` helpers (and `requests`) to be runnable from the repo root.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`stock_fundamental.py`](#stock_fundamentalpy): one trailing Yahoo snapshot, quarterly YoY growth, no interest coverage / ROIC / buyback data, often-missing PEG.

---

## stock_earnings_calendar.py

Pulls a stock's earnings calendar from Yahoo Finance's public `quoteSummary` endpoint: when it next reports (with the analyst EPS and revenue estimates for that quarter), its ex-dividend and payment dates, and its last four quarters of EPS against estimates. No API key or authentication required — the cookie + crumb handshake and the fetch are [`yahoo_finance.fetch_quote_summary()`](#yahoo_financepy).

| Section | What it shows | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Next earnings | date and UTC time (or Yahoo's estimated window), confirmed vs estimated, upcoming vs past; EPS and revenue estimate low / average / high | `calendarEvents.earnings` |
| Dividend | ex-dividend and payment dates, each upcoming vs past; the last dividend per share and its ex-date | `calendarEvents.exDividendDate`, `dividendDate`; `defaultKeyStatistics.lastDividendValue`, `lastDividendDate` |
| Earnings history | up to four quarters: quarter end, fiscal quarter, report date, EPS estimate, actual, surprise | `earningsHistory`; report date and fiscal-quarter label from `earnings.earningsChart` |

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `yahoo_finance.py` (the fetch), `stock_intrinsic_value.py` (number formatting) and `cli_utils.py` — run from the repo root so they import

### Usage

```bash
python3 stock_earnings_calendar.py SYMBOL [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SYMBOL` (positional) | — | Ticker in Yahoo notation: `AAPL`, `VOD.L`, `D05.SI`, `SAP.DE`, … |
| `--json` | off | Emit structured JSON on stdout instead of the report |

#### Example

```bash
python3 stock_earnings_calendar.py AAPL
```

```
AAPL - Apple Inc.  (NasdaqGS - USD)

Next earnings
  Date               2026-11-02 20:00 UTC  (confirmed, upcoming)
  EPS estimate       1.98  (range 1.93 - 2.07) USD
  Revenue estimate   113.61B  (range 112.25B - 117.22B) USD

Dividend
  Ex-dividend date   2026-08-10  (past)
  Payment date       2026-08-13  (past)
  Last dividend      0.27 USD  (ex-date 2026-08-10)

Earnings history (EPS, USD)
  Quarter end  Fiscal Q  Reported     Estimate    Actual  Surprise
  2025-09-30   4Q2025    2025-10-30       1.77      1.85    +4.52%
  2025-12-31   1Q2026    2026-01-29       2.67      2.84    +6.34%
  2026-03-31   2Q2026    2026-04-30       1.94      2.01    +3.46%
  2026-06-30   3Q2026    2026-07-30       1.89      2.02    +6.74%
```

(Figures move with live data; the closing notes are omitted.) With `--json`: `{symbol, name, exchange, currency, reporting_currency, next_earnings, dividend, earnings_history}`:

- `next_earnings` — `{date, date_end, datetime_utc, is_estimate, status, eps_estimate, revenue_estimate}` or `null`; each estimate is `{low, average, high}` or `null`.
- `dividend` — `{ex_dividend_date, ex_dividend_status, payment_date, payment_status, last_amount, last_amount_date, amount_currency}` or `null`.
- `earnings_history` — oldest first, `[{quarter_end, fiscal_quarter, reported_date, eps_estimate, eps_actual, surprise_pct, currency}, ...]`; `surprise_pct` is a fraction (`0.0452` = +4.52%).

### How it works

1. **Fetch** — `yahoo_finance.fetch_quote_summary()`: the cookie + crumb handshake, then `quoteSummary` for `price,financialData,defaultKeyStatistics,calendarEvents,earningsHistory,earnings`.
2. **Next earnings** — `next_earnings()` sorts `calendarEvents.earnings.earningsDate`. One timestamp is a confirmed date; two are Yahoo's estimated window (`date` → `date_end`). `earningsCallDate` is ignored — it is often stale.
3. **Dividend** — `dividend_dates()` reads the ex-dividend and payment dates and the last dividend paid.
4. **History** — `earnings_history()` reads `earningsHistory` and matches each quarter, by quarter-end date, to the `earnings` chart entry carrying its report date and fiscal-quarter label.
5. **Status** — every date is compared with today's UTC date: on or after → `upcoming`, before → `past`.
6. **Report** — the three sections, or the JSON object.

### API reference

- `MODULES` — The `quoteSummary` modules fetched.
- `next_earnings(modules, today)`, `dividend_dates(modules, today, currency)`, `earnings_history(modules)` — The three sections from the raw modules.
- `extract_calendar(modules, today=None)` — Returns the JSON-shaped dict (without `symbol`); `today` defaults to the current UTC date.
- `build_report(symbol, calendar)` / `build_json(symbol, calendar)` — The text report and the JSON payload.
- `parse_args(argv=None)`, `main(argv=None)` — The CLI.

### Error handling

- Network failure or a bad ticker → exits 1, printing `Error: …` to stderr (or `{"error": "…"}` with `--json`).
- A symbol with no next earnings date **and** no earnings history — an ETF, index, currency or future — exits 1 with `no earnings data for '<symbol>' (only company stocks have earnings)`.
- A missing symbol or unknown flag is rejected by argument parsing (exit code 2), before any request is made.

### Notes / limitations

- Yahoo keeps the last dividend's dates until the next ones are announced, so the ex-dividend date is often in the past — check its status. The next earnings date can briefly be in the past too, right after a report.
- Earnings times are UTC; the date shown is the UTC date, which can differ from the exchange-local date for a report near midnight UTC.
- EPS and revenue estimates are in the **reporting** currency (`financialData.financialCurrency`), which can differ from the trading currency: `VOD.L` trades in `GBp` but reports in `EUR`.
- For minor-unit listings (`GBp`, `ILA`, `ZAc`) the dividend amount is reported without a currency: Yahoo returns it in pounds or the dividend's declared currency (BP and HSBC come back in USD), not pence, and doesn't say which.
- Coverage varies outside the US — `VOD.L` has a date but no estimates or history. Same undocumented endpoint and fragile crumb/cookie flow as `stock_intrinsic_value.py`.

---

## Claude Code agent: `stock-earnings-calendar`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/stock-earnings-calendar.md`. Like the other agents here, it calls no API itself — it shells out via the `Bash` tool to [`stock_earnings_calendar.py`](#stock_earnings_calendarpy).

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer requests like "when does Apple report earnings?", "what EPS is expected for NVDA this quarter?", "did DBS beat estimates last quarter?" or "when is Microsoft's ex-dividend date?" from live Yahoo Finance data, instead of stale training data.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for questions about a named company's upcoming or past earnings announcements, EPS or revenue estimates, earnings surprises, or dividend dates, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter. Price targets go to [`stock-analyst-targets`](#claude-code-agent-stock-analyst-targets) instead.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "stock-earnings-calendar"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Resolves the company to a Yahoo ticker, adding the exchange suffix for non-US listings; if the ticker is uncertain it says so rather than guessing. It declines ETFs, indices, currencies and commodities (no earnings) without running the script, and runs once per ticker for several companies.
2. Runs `python3 stock_earnings_calendar.py SYMBOL --json` from the repo root.
3. Answers the question asked first: the next report date (time in UTC if confirmed; confirmed vs estimated window) with the EPS / revenue consensus; or the latest quarter's beat or miss and the pattern across quarters; or the dividend dates — saying the next ex-dividend date isn't announced yet when the one Yahoo has is past. Several companies go in one table sorted by report date.
4. Closes with a caveat that dates and estimates can change and should be checked against the company's investor-relations page. On failure it surfaces the `{"error": "..."}` from stderr and retries at most once.

### Configuration

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`stock_earnings_calendar.py`](#stock_earnings_calendarpy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Requires `stock_earnings_calendar.py` and its `yahoo_finance.py` / `stock_intrinsic_value.py` / `cli_utils.py` helpers (and `requests`) to be runnable from the repo root.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`stock_earnings_calendar.py`](#stock_earnings_calendarpy): stale dividend dates until new ones are announced, UTC dates, thinner coverage outside the US.
