# Tech News & Discussions

---

## hn.py

Shared helper module for the Hacker News scripts: [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy), [`hn_search.py`](#hn_searchpy) and [`hn_thread.py`](#hn_threadpy). It holds what they have in common — JSON fetching, fetching one item from the official [Hacker News API](https://github.com/HackerNews/API), the item's HN page link, its posting time, and converting HN's HTML comment and self-post text to plain text — so links, times and text look the same in all three. Each script keeps its own endpoints, record shaping and output stage. Not a CLI — it is imported, not run.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)

### Configuration

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `HN_API_BASE` | `https://hacker-news.firebaseio.com/v0` | Official Hacker News API base URL (`hottest_tech_discussions.py` builds `topstories.json` from it) |
| `HN_ITEM_URL` | `{HN_API_BASE}/item/{item_id}.json` | One item from the official API, used by `fetch_story()` |
| `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` | 10 | Default request timeout in seconds for `fetch_json()` |
| `TEXT_CHARS` | 600 | Default length `clean_text()` cuts text to; also the default for `--max-chars` in `hn_search.py` and `hn_thread.py` |

### API reference

- `fetch_json(url, params=None, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT)` — GETs the URL and returns parsed JSON. On an error status it raises `requests.HTTPError`; when the Algolia API explains a rejected request (`{"message": ...}`), the error is `"<status>: <message>"` rather than a bare status line. Used by all three scripts (`hn_thread.py` passes a 30-second timeout for big comment trees).
- `fetch_story(item_id)` — One item from the official HN API, or `None` if the request or JSON parsing fails (so one bad item doesn't sink a batch) or the item doesn't exist. Used by `hottest_tech_discussions.py` for story details and by `hn_thread.py` for HN's ranked comment order (an item's `kids`).
- `parse_item_id(value)` — An item id from a number (`48843352`) or an HN item link (`https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48843352`, other query parameters allowed); raises `ValueError` otherwise. Used by `hn_thread.py`'s `ITEM` and `hn_search.py`'s `--story`.
- `discussion_url(story)` — The `https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=<id>` page for an item (any dict with an `id`).
- `posted_at(story)` — An item's `time` (Unix seconds) as `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM UTC`; a missing time gives the Unix epoch.
- `clean_text(raw, max_chars=TEXT_CHARS)` — Plain text from HN's HTML: `<p>` becomes a blank line, other tags are dropped (a link keeps its visible text), entities are decoded, extra blank lines are collapsed, and the result is cut to `max_chars` with `...` (0 = no limit). Returns `None` for empty input.

### Notes / limitations

- `fetch_story()` hides *why* an item couldn't be fetched; callers that need to tell failure from a missing item should use `fetch_json()` directly.
- `clean_text()` is a regex-based cleanup for HN's small HTML subset (`<p>`, `<i>`, `<a>`, `<pre><code>`), not a general HTML parser. Code blocks lose their `<pre>` formatting but keep their line breaks.
- Changing a helper here changes the output of all three scripts.

---

## hottest_tech_discussions.py

Prints the hottest technology discussions (10 by default, set with `--limit`) currently on [Hacker News](https://news.ycombinator.com/), using the public [Hacker News API](https://github.com/HackerNews/API). No API key or authentication required.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `hn.py` and `cli_utils.py` modules (no install — run from the repo root so they import)

```bash
pip install requests
```

### Usage

```bash
python3 hottest_tech_discussions.py [--limit N] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `--limit N` | 10 | Number of stories to return (must be 1 or more) |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report — for calling this script as a tool from an agent or another program |

#### Example output

```
Fetching the hottest technology discussions from Hacker News...

1. Some Story Title
   Score: 512  |  Comments: 234  |  Posted: 2026-08-21 09:15 UTC
   Link: https://example.com/article
   Discussion: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=12345678

2. Another Story Title
   Score: 480  |  Comments: 190  |  Posted: 2026-08-21 07:42 UTC
   Link: https://example.com/other-article
   Discussion: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=12345679

...
```

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 hottest_tech_discussions.py --json --limit 2
```

```json
[
  {
    "rank": 1,
    "title": "Some Story Title",
    "score": 512,
    "comments": 234,
    "posted": "2026-08-21 09:15 UTC",
    "url": "https://example.com/article",
    "discussion_url": "https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=12345678"
  },
  {
    "rank": 2,
    "title": "Another Story Title",
    "score": 480,
    "comments": 190,
    "posted": "2026-08-21 07:42 UTC",
    "url": "https://example.com/other-article",
    "discussion_url": "https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=12345679"
  }
]
```

With `--json`, the leading progress line is suppressed and results print as a JSON array on stdout. On failure, an exit code of `1` is returned and a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) is printed to stderr instead of plain text.

### How it works

1. **Fetch candidate pool** — Calls `topstories.json` on the HN API to get the current top story IDs (already ranked by HN's own hotness algorithm) and takes the first `limit × CANDIDATE_POOL_FACTOR` of them (4× the requested count: 40 for the default 10, 200 for `--limit 50`).
2. **Fetch story details concurrently** — Uses a `ThreadPoolExecutor` (10 workers) to fetch the full item data (`item/{id}.json`) for each candidate in parallel, since each is a separate HTTP request.
3. **Filter and rank** — Discards anything that failed to fetch or isn't a `story` (e.g. jobs, polls get excluded implicitly since only `type == "story"` is kept), then sorts the remaining stories by `score` descending.
4. **Display top N** — Prints the top `--limit` stories (default 10, `RESULTS_TO_SHOW`), each with rank, title, score, comment count, posting time (UTC), the external article link, and a link to the HN discussion thread.

### Configuration

These are set as constants near the top of the file — edit them directly to change behavior:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `CANDIDATE_POOL_FACTOR` | 4 | Top stories to fetch details for, per requested result, before ranking (pool = `limit × 4`) |
| `RESULTS_TO_SHOW` | 10 | Default for `--limit` (number of stories to display) |

The request timeout (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, 10 seconds) and the API base URL live in [`hn.py`](#hnpy).

### API reference

- Functions and script structure are documented via a docstring at the top of the file and inline comments explaining non-obvious choices (e.g. why the candidate pool is 4× the limit).
- `get_hottest_tech_discussions(limit=10)` — Orchestrates fetching, filtering, and ranking; returns a list of raw HN story dicts.
- `fetch_json`, `fetch_story`, `discussion_url` and `posted_at` come from [`hn.py`](#hnpy); `fetch_story()` returns `None` for a story that fails to fetch, so one bad story doesn't kill the whole batch. [`stock_tech_buzz_agent.py`](#stock_tech_buzz_agentpy) uses `discussion_url` through this module.
- `story_to_dict(rank, story)` — Flattens one raw story into the `{rank, title, score, comments, posted, url, discussion_url}` record used for **both** output modes.
- `format_story(row)` — Renders a `story_to_dict()` record as a multi-line human-readable string.
- `parse_args(argv=None)` — Parses `--limit` and `--json` CLI flags.
- `main(argv=None)` — Entry point; fetches, handles top-level network errors, and prints results (either human-readable or JSON, depending on `--json`).

### Error handling

- If the initial `topstories.json` request fails (network error, timeout, non-2xx response), the script prints an error and exits with status code 1 — plain text on stderr normally, or a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) on stderr when `--json` is passed.
- If an individual story's detail fetch fails, that story is silently dropped from consideration rather than aborting the whole run.
- A `--limit` that isn't a positive integer is rejected by argparse (exit code 2) before any network call.
- If no stories are found after filtering, the script prints `"No stories found."` (or `[]` with `--json`) and exits normally.

### Notes / limitations

- "Hottest" is defined here as *highest score* among HN's current top stories — it does not itself factor in comment velocity or recency beyond what HN's own top-stories ranking already provides.
- No filtering is applied for "tech" specifically — it relies on Hacker News' general subject matter (predominantly tech) rather than keyword filtering.
- Results reflect a live snapshot; scores and rankings will differ between runs.
- Hacker News is the only source. Lobsters (lobste.rs) was supported from 8dbf161 to 107e24a (2026-10-19) and then removed: its robots.txt asks every client other than a few named search engines to stay off the whole site (`User-agent: *` / `Disallow: /`, with a comment asking people to follow the standard honestly), disallows `/search` and `/page/` even for those engines, and declares `Content-Signal: ai-input=no, ai-train=no`. The repo's scripts fetched its JSON feeds and the agents fed the content to an AI model, both against those wishes. See [`docs/things-to-explore.md`](docs/things-to-explore.md) before adding another source: check its robots.txt and terms first.

---

## Claude Code agent: `hottest-tech-discussions`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/hottest-tech-discussions.md`. It doesn't call any external API itself — it shells out to [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy) via the `Bash` tool and reports the results conversationally.

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer questions like "what's trending in tech today?" or "top HN discussions" by running the script and summarizing its JSON output, instead of guessing or using stale training data.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own for tech-news/trends questions, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter. Questions about what HN has said about a topic over time go to [`hn-search`](#claude-code-agent-hn-search).
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "hottest-tech-discussions"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Runs `python3 hottest_tech_discussions.py --json --limit N` (default `N=10`, or whatever count the user asked for) from the repo root.
2. Parses the JSON array of stories.
3. Reports them as a concise list — title, score, comment count, and links — without fabricating data. If the command fails, it surfaces the `{"error": "..."}` from stderr instead of retrying repeatedly or inventing results.

### Configuration

The agent's frontmatter restricts it to the `Bash` tool only, since running the script and reading its stdout is all it needs.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying script | [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Requires `hottest_tech_discussions.py` (and its `requests` dependency) to be present and runnable from the repo root.
- Inherits all the limitations of [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy) itself (score-based "hottest" definition, no tech-specific keyword filtering, live-snapshot results).

---

## hn_search.py

Searches past [Hacker News](https://news.ycombinator.com/) stories and comments for a query, using the [Algolia HN Search API](https://hn.algolia.com/api) — the same index behind the search box on HN. Where [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy) only sees the current front page, this searches the whole archive: stories (or just Ask HN / Show HN posts) or comments, ranked by relevance or newest first, optionally limited to a date window, a minimum score or one author. HN's monthly job threads ("Who is hiring?", "Who wants to be hired?", "Freelancer? Seeking freelancer?") are left out by default, since their comments list technologies as skills rather than discussing them. No API key or authentication required.

Requires [`hn.py`](#hnpy) to be present in the same directory — it imports `fetch_json`, `clean_text`, `discussion_url`, `posted_at` and `TEXT_CHARS` from it, so links, times and text look the same as in the other HN scripts. It also imports `die`, `positive_int` and `non_negative_int` from [`cli_utils.py`](#cli_utilspy).

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `hn.py` and `cli_utils.py` modules (no install — run from the repo root so they import)

### Usage

```bash
python3 hn_search.py QUERY [--type {story,comment,ask,show}] [--sort {relevance,date}] [--since YYYY-MM-DD] [--until YYYY-MM-DD] [--min-points N] [--author USERNAME] [--story ITEM] [--include-job-threads] [--limit N] [--max-chars N] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `QUERY` (positional) | — | Words to search for, e.g. `"crowdstrike outage"`. Every word must match, as a whole word (see Notes / limitations below) |
| `--type` | `story` | `story`, `comment`, `ask` (Ask HN posts) or `show` (Show HN posts) |
| `--sort` | `relevance` | `relevance` (best match, then score) or `date` (newest first) |
| `--since` | — | Only items posted on or after this UTC date |
| `--until` | — | Only items posted on or before this UTC date |
| `--min-points N` | — | Only stories with a score of at least `N`. Not allowed with `--type comment` (comments have no public score) |
| `--author USERNAME` | — | Only items posted by this HN user |
| `--story ITEM` | — | Only comments in one story's thread: the story's id or HN link. Implies `--type comment` (any other `--type` is an error). Pairs with [`hn_thread.py`](#hn_threadpy), which reads a thread's top comments — this searches all of them |
| `--include-job-threads` | off | Keep the monthly job threads and their comments, which are left out by default (see How it works). Has no effect with `--story` |
| `--limit N` | 10 | Number of results, 1-100 |
| `--max-chars N` | 600 | Cut comment / self-post text to `N` characters (with `...`); `0` keeps it whole |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report |

#### Example output

```bash
python3 hn_search.py "bun" --limit 2
```

```
Searching Hacker News for 'bun'...

777 matching stories; showing 2, by relevance.

1. Anthropic acquires Bun
   Score: 2192  |  Comments: 1072  |  Posted: 2025-12-02 18:05 UTC  |  By: ryanvogel
   Link: https://bun.com/blog/bun-joins-anthropic
   Discussion: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=46124267

2. Bun: Fast JavaScript runtime, transpiler, and NPM client written in Zig
   Score: 1431  |  Comments: 314  |  Posted: 2022-07-05 20:41 UTC  |  By: firloop
   Link: https://bun.sh/?launch
   Discussion: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=31993429
```

A comment search shows who said what, on which story:

```bash
python3 hn_search.py "crowdstrike outage" --type comment --since 2024-07-19 --until 2024-07-20 --limit 1 --max-chars 200
```

```
Searching Hacker News for 'crowdstrike outage'...

53 matching comments; showing 1, by relevance.

1. Comment by jeroenhd on "Microsoft's global sprawl under fire from regulators after Windows outage"
   Posted: 2024-07-20 21:46 UTC
   Comment: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=41020260
   Discussion: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=41019478

   Part of Microsoft's problem is that right before the CrowdStrike outage, there was an outage of some large Microsoft services, making the two look connected. It didn't help that a lot of Microsoft sup...
```

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 hn_search.py "rust" --type show --sort date --min-points 100 --limit 1 --json
```

```json
{
  "query": "rust",
  "type": "show",
  "sort": "date",
  "since": null,
  "until": null,
  "story": null,
  "total_matches": 233,
  "job_threads_skipped": 0,
  "results": [
    {
      "rank": 1,
      "type": "story",
      "title": "Show HN: SolidCraft – Open-Source Parametric CAD in Rust (With MCP Support)",
      "author": "bherbruck",
      "posted": "2026-10-13 00:11 UTC",
      "score": 140,
      "comments": 51,
      "url": "https://github.com/bherbruck/solidcraft",
      "discussion_url": "https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50064840",
      "text": null
    }
  ]
}
```

`total_matches` counts every match, not just the results returned, minus the job threads that were left out. `job_threads_skipped` is how many job threads were left out (`0` with `--include-job-threads` or `--story`). The text report adds a line such as `(Left out 3 job threads; --include-job-threads keeps them.)`. Each result has the same keys:

- **Stories** — `url` is the linked article, or the HN thread for a self-post (Ask HN, most Show HN); `text` is a self-post's body, or `null`.
- **Comments** — `type` is `"comment"`, `title` is the story the comment is on, `url` is the comment's own HN page, `discussion_url` the story's thread, `score` and `comments` are `null`, and `text` is the comment.

`story` is the `--story` id, or `null`.

#### Searching one thread (`--story`)

```bash
python3 hn_search.py fuzzing --story https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48843352 --limit 1 --max-chars 200
```

```
Searching Hacker News for 'fuzzing'...

23 matching comments in thread 48843352; showing 1, by relevance.

1. Comment by lolinder on "My thoughts on the Bun Rust rewrite"
   Posted: 2026-07-13 13:56 UTC
   Comment: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48892765
   Discussion: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48843352

   If that's what he meant that doesn't speak well for his communication in this post, because "the Bun team told us that they were not fuzzing anything" is a wildly different claim than "the Bun team to...
```

`--story` must be the **story's** id: Algolia files every comment under its story, so a comment's id matches nothing. When a `--story` search finds nothing, the report says so.

With `--json`, the leading progress line is suppressed. On failure, an exit code of `1` is returned and a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) is printed to stderr instead of plain text.

### How it works

1. **Validate** — Rejects an empty query, a bad date, `--until` before `--since`, `--min-points` with `--type comment` (or `--story`), a `--story` that isn't an id or HN link or is combined with a `--type` other than `comment`, a `--limit` outside 1-100, or an invalid username, before any request.
2. **Build the query** — `build_params()` maps `--type` to an Algolia tag (`story`, `comment`, `ask_hn`, `show_hn`), adds `author_<USERNAME>` for `--author` and `story_<ITEM>` for `--story`, a negative `-story_<ID>` tag for each job thread being left out, turns `--since` / `--until` into `created_at_i` bounds (00:00 UTC on `--since` up to, but not including, 00:00 UTC the day after `--until`) and `--min-points` into `points>=N`, and sets `typoTolerance=false` and `queryType=prefixNone` so words match whole.
3. **Fetch** — GETs `hn.algolia.com/api/v1/search` (relevance) or `/search_by_date` (newest first) with `hitsPerPage` = `--limit`.
   - **Leaving out job threads** (unless `--include-job-threads` or `--story`). Algolia can't filter by thread title, and the ~500 job threads since 2010 are too many to exclude in one URL (it fails at about 200). Instead `search()` checks the results' thread titles against `JOB_THREAD_TITLE`, excludes any job threads it finds by id (`-story_<ID>`), and searches again. It stops when a page has none, or after `MAX_JOB_ROUNDS` (5) searches, then drops any job-thread results still left, which can return fewer than `--limit`. A search's job hits come from only a few threads, so this converges quickly. Measured on comment searches for `swift`, `swift ios`, `golang`, `rust`, `react` and `kubernetes` at `--limit` 10 and 50, a clean page took 2 searches, 4 at most (1–19 threads excluded). A story search mostly for the job threads themselves (`"who is hiring"`) runs out of rounds and returns few results; use `--include-job-threads` for that.
4. **Shape** — `hit_to_dict()` turns each hit into a story or comment record; `hn.clean_text()` converts the comment / self-post HTML to plain text (paragraphs to blank lines, tags dropped, entities decoded) and cuts it to `--max-chars`.

### Configuration

These are set as constants near the top of the file — edit them directly to change behavior:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SEARCH_URL` | `https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/search` | Endpoint for `--sort relevance` |
| `SEARCH_BY_DATE_URL` | `https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/search_by_date` | Endpoint for `--sort date` |
| `RESULTS_TO_SHOW` | 10 | Default for `--limit` |
| `MAX_RESULTS` | 100 | Upper bound accepted for `--limit` |
| `TYPE_TAGS` | `story`/`comment`/`ask`/`show` → Algolia tag | `--type` choices and the tags they search |
| `JOB_THREAD_TITLE` | regex for "Ask HN: Who is hiring?", "Who wants to be hired?", "Freelancer(s)? Seeking freelancer(s)?" | Titles of the job threads left out by default, case-insensitive, allowing a leading space |
| `MAX_JOB_ROUNDS` | 5 | Most searches run while job threads keep turning up |

The request timeout (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, 10 seconds) and the `--max-chars` default (`TEXT_CHARS`, 600) live in [`hn.py`](#hnpy).

### API reference

- `build_params(query, kind="story", since=None, until=None, min_points=None, author=None, limit=10, story=None, exclude_stories=())` — The Algolia query parameters for a search; `exclude_stories` leaves out those stories and their comments.
- `search(query, kind="story", sort="relevance", since=None, until=None, min_points=None, author=None, limit=10, story=None, include_job_threads=False)` — Runs a search, leaving out job threads unless `include_job_threads` or `story` is set; returns `(hits, total_matches, job_threads_skipped)`.
- `is_job_thread(title)` / `is_job_hit(hit)` — Whether a title is one of the job threads, and whether a story or comment hit is (in) one.
- `fetch_json` (which surfaces Algolia's own error message) and `clean_text` come from [`hn.py`](#hnpy).
- `hit_to_dict(rank, hit, max_chars=600)` — Flattens an Algolia hit into the `{rank, type, title, author, posted, score, comments, url, discussion_url, text}` record used for **both** output modes.
- `format_hit(row)` — Renders a `hit_to_dict()` record as a multi-line human-readable string.
- `parse_args(argv=None)` — Parses and validates the CLI arguments (exit 2 on invalid ones).
- `main(argv=None)` — Entry point; searches, handles top-level errors, and prints the results (human-readable or JSON).

### Error handling

- Invalid arguments — see step 1 of How it works above — are rejected by argument parsing (exit code 2), before any request.
- If the request fails (network error, timeout, non-2xx response, non-JSON body), the script exits 1 with the error on stderr (`{"error": "..."}` with `--json`). When Algolia rejects a request, its own message is the error.
- No matches is not an error: the script prints `No results found.` (or an empty `results` list with `--json`) and exits 0. With `--story`, the report adds that the id must be a story's, not a comment's. If job threads were left out, the report says so even when nothing else matched.

### Notes / limitations

- **Whole-word matching.** Typo tolerance and prefix matching are turned off, so `"bun"` doesn't also match "Bunch", "bundle" or "build" (with Algolia's defaults it does — 196,449 story matches for "bun" against 777). The flip side: `"kubernetes"` won't find "k8s", and a plural or other word form may need its own search. Every query word must match (in the title, URL, text or author).
- **A word matches in any sense.** A comment search for `"swift"` also finds "a swift recovery", Taylor Swift and job posts listing Swift as a skill. In 50-comment samples (October 2026), 23 of `swift`'s relevance-sorted results came from the monthly "Who is hiring?" / "Who wants to be hired?" threads; the script now leaves those threads out by default. Other senses of the word, and a user whose name is the query word, still match. The script doesn't filter those; the [`hn-search`](#claude-code-agent-hn-search) agent does.
- **`total_matches` is approximate with job threads left out.** It no longer counts the excluded threads, but still counts any job thread that never appeared in the results. Algolia's counts for `--sort date` also vary noticeably from one request to the next. Leaving out job threads costs one extra request per round (usually one, at most four more).
- **"Relevance" ranks by text match first, then points.** A single-word query tends to put the biggest threads first; a multi-word query can rank a small story that matches all the words closely above a popular one. Use `--sort date` with `--min-points` for "the latest notable threads".
- Comments have no public score in the API, hence no `--min-points` for them; comment results can't be ranked by popularity. A comment search by `relevance` therefore ties on the word match and returns an arbitrary slice rather than the best comments: on 2026-10-21, `"zig"` and `"kubernetes"` came back as comments from 2015 only, and `--story 41002195` (the CrowdStrike outage thread) as its newest comments. For comments, `--sort date` with `--since` / `--until` picks the period deliberately; the `hn-search` agent is told to do that.
- Story scores and comment counts are as of Algolia's last update of that item, which can lag HN slightly for recent stories.
- Algolia returns at most 1,000 hits per request; this script caps `--limit` at 100 and doesn't page. "Who is hiring right now?" (an extra thread from March 2020) isn't treated as a job thread, since its title doesn't match the monthly pattern.
- Links inside a comment keep their visible text only, which HN itself shortens for long URLs.
- `--story` can't tell a comment's id from a story with no matching comments — both return nothing.

---

## hn_thread.py

Reads one [Hacker News](https://news.ycombinator.com/) thread — a story (or a comment) and its comments — in the order HN itself ranks them, trimmed to a readable size: the top N top-level comments, a few replies under each, down to a set depth. Built for summarizing a big discussion found with [`hn_search.py`](#hn_searchpy). Uses the [Algolia HN API](https://hn.algolia.com/api) for the comment tree and the official [Hacker News API](https://github.com/HackerNews/API) for the ranking. No API key or authentication required.

Requires [`hn.py`](#hnpy) to be present in the same directory — it imports `fetch_json`, `fetch_story`, `parse_item_id`, `clean_text`, `discussion_url`, `posted_at` and `TEXT_CHARS` from it. It also imports `die`, `bounded_int` and `non_negative_int` from [`cli_utils.py`](#cli_utilspy).

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `hn.py` and `cli_utils.py` modules (no install — run from the repo root so they import)

### Usage

```bash
python3 hn_thread.py ITEM [--top N] [--replies N] [--depth N] [--max-chars N] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `ITEM` (positional) | — | An HN item id (`48843352`) or link (`https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48843352`). Usually a story; a comment id reads that comment and its replies |
| `--top N` | 20 | Top-level comments to show, 1-200 |
| `--replies N` | 3 | Replies to show under each shown comment (0 for none) |
| `--depth N` | 2 | Comment levels to show: 1 = top-level only, 2 = plus replies, … up to 10 |
| `--max-chars N` | 600 | Cut each comment to `N` characters (with `...`); `0` keeps them whole |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report |

With the defaults that's at most 20 + 20 × 3 = 80 comments; `--depth 3` adds up to 3 replies under each of those replies.

#### Example output

```bash
python3 hn_thread.py https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48843352 --top 2 --replies 1 --max-chars 160
```

```
Reading Hacker News thread 48843352...

My thoughts on the Bun Rust rewrite
   Score: 817  |  Comments: 683  |  Posted: 2026-07-09 09:47 UTC  |  By: kristoff_it
   Link: https://andrewkelley.me/post/my-thoughts-bun-rust-rewrite.html
   Discussion: https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48843352

Showing 4 of 683 comments (96 top-level), in HN's ranked order.

   1. tuckwat | 2026-07-09 11:48 UTC | https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48844355
      This is unprofessional and embarrassing for Zig.

      I know very little about Jared but his article yesterday, which I read, seemed appreciative of Zig. I now lear...

      1.1. 0xpgm | 2026-07-09 12:30 UTC | https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48844834
         How is it unprofessional when it is simply someone giving their honest personal opinion on an issue that involves something that is valuable to them, on their p...
         (+6 more replies, 48 comments below this one in all)
      (+26 more replies, 244 comments below this one in all)

   2. Jarred | 2026-07-09 13:38 UTC | https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48845652
      I have learned so much reading Andrew’s code and as I said in the original post: Bun would never have  happened without Zig.
      ...
```

The `(+N more replies, M comments below this one in all)` lines say what was left out under a comment.

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 hn_thread.py 48843352 --top 1 --replies 0 --max-chars 50 --json
```

```json
{
  "id": 48843352,
  "type": "story",
  "title": "My thoughts on the Bun Rust rewrite",
  "author": "kristoff_it",
  "posted": "2026-07-09 09:47 UTC",
  "score": 817,
  "url": "https://andrewkelley.me/post/my-thoughts-bun-rust-rewrite.html",
  "discussion_url": "https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48843352",
  "story_url": "https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48843352",
  "text": null,
  "total_comments": 683,
  "top_level_comments": 96,
  "shown_comments": 1,
  "ranked": true,
  "comments": [
    {
      "id": 48844355,
      "author": "tuckwat",
      "posted": "2026-07-09 11:48 UTC",
      "depth": 1,
      "url": "https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48844355",
      "text": "This is unprofessional and embarrassing for Zig....",
      "reply_count": 27,
      "total_replies": 244,
      "replies": []
    }
  ]
}
```

- `comments` is a tree: each comment's `replies` holds the replies shown under it, with the same keys. `reply_count` is how many direct replies it has and `total_replies` how many comments sit below it at any depth, so the caller can tell what was trimmed.
- `ranked` is `false` if HN's ranked order couldn't be fetched for some comment, whose replies are then oldest first.
- `url` is the story's article (or the HN page for a self-post or comment); `story_url` is the story's HN thread, which differs from `discussion_url` when `ITEM` is a comment. `text` is a self-post's or root comment's body, else `null`. For a comment, `title` and `score` are `null`.
- A deleted comment that still has replies is shown with author and text `[deleted]`; one with no replies is left out.

With `--json`, the leading progress line is suppressed. On failure, an exit code of `1` is returned and a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) is printed to stderr instead of plain text.

### How it works

1. **Parse the item** — Accepts a numeric id or a `news.ycombinator.com/item?id=N` link; anything else exits 2.
2. **Fetch the tree** — One request to `hn.algolia.com/api/v1/items/<id>` returns the item with its whole comment tree under `children` (around 500 KB and 2-3 s for a 1,000-comment thread, hence a 30-second timeout). A `404` means there is no such item.
3. **Rank** — Algolia lists replies oldest first, but HN ranks them. The official API's `kids` list for an item is in HN's ranked display order, so `select_comments()` fetches it (via `hn.fetch_story()`, 10 at a time) for the root and then, level by level, for each shown comment that has replies. Only those items are fetched, not the whole thread. If one fails, that comment's replies stay oldest first and `ranked` becomes `false`. Comments Algolia has but `kids` doesn't (e.g. just posted) go last.
4. **Trim** — Keeps the first `--top` top-level comments, then the first `--replies` replies under each, down to `--depth` levels. Deleted or dead comments with no replies are dropped.
5. **Shape** — Converts each comment's HTML to plain text with `hn.clean_text()`, cut to `--max-chars`, and counts what was left out.

### Configuration

These are set as constants near the top of the file — edit them directly to change behavior:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `ITEM_URL` | `https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/items/{item_id}` | Algolia item endpoint (the comment tree) |
| `ITEM_TIMEOUT` | 30 | Timeout in seconds for the tree request |
| `TOP_COMMENTS` | 20 | Default for `--top` |
| `REPLIES_PER_COMMENT` | 3 | Default for `--replies` |
| `DEPTH` | 2 | Default for `--depth` |
| `MAX_TOP_COMMENTS` | 200 | Upper bound for `--top` |
| `MAX_DEPTH` | 10 | Upper bound for `--depth` |

The ranking requests use [`hn.py`](#hnpy)'s 10-second `REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, and the `--max-chars` default is its `TEXT_CHARS`.

### API reference

- `parse_item_id` (an id from a number or HN link) comes from [`hn.py`](#hnpy).
- `fetch_tree(item_id)` — The item and its comment tree from Algolia, or `None` for a `404`.
- `count_comments(node)` — Comments below a node, at any depth.
- `is_empty(node)` — True for a deleted or dead comment with no replies.
- `ranked_children(node, kids)` — A node's non-empty children in the order of `kids` (HN's ranked list), or oldest first if `kids` is `None`.
- `fetch_kids(item_ids)` — `{id: ranked kids list, or None if the item couldn't be fetched}`, 10 requests at a time.
- `select_comments(root, top=20, replies=3, depth=2)` — Picks the comments to show; returns `(trees, ranked)` with `trees` as `(node, level, shown_children)` tuples.
- `comment_to_dict(tree, max_chars=600)` / `thread_to_dict(root, comments, ranked, max_chars=600)` — The records used for **both** output modes.
- `format_comment(row, number)` / `format_thread(thread)` — Render the human-readable report, numbering replies `1.1.`, `1.2.` and so on.
- `parse_args(argv=None)` — Parses and validates the CLI arguments (exit 2 on invalid ones).
- `main(argv=None)` — Entry point; fetches, ranks, trims, handles errors, and prints the thread.

### Error handling

- An `ITEM` that isn't an id or HN item link, or an out-of-range `--top` / `--replies` / `--depth` / `--max-chars`, is rejected by argument parsing (exit code 2) before any request.
- No such item: exits 1 with `No Hacker News item with id <ID>.`
- If the tree request fails (network error, timeout, other error status, non-JSON body), the script exits 1 with the error on stderr (`{"error": "..."}` with `--json`).
- A failed ranking request doesn't fail the script; it only sets `ranked` to `false` (see How it works, step 3).

### Notes / limitations

- **"Top" means HN's ranking**, which weighs votes, age and moderation — the order you see on the site. Comment scores aren't public, so there's no "most upvoted" sort.
- **A sample of a big thread.** With the defaults, a 700-comment thread is read as at most 80 comments, the highest-ranked ones; the trimmed-comment counts show how much was skipped. Raise `--top`, `--replies` or `--depth` for more, at the cost of more ranking requests (one per shown comment with replies).
- `total_comments` counts the comments in Algolia's tree, which can be a little lower than HN's own count (HN includes some deleted and dead comments).
- Links inside a comment keep their visible text only, which HN shortens for long URLs.

---

## Claude Code agent: `hn-search`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/hn-search.md`. It calls no API itself — it shells out to [`hn_search.py`](#hn_searchpy) and [`hn_thread.py`](#hn_threadpy) via the `Bash` tool and reports the result conversationally.

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer questions like "what does HN think of Bun?", "find HN threads about the CrowdStrike outage", "what did people on Hacker News say about Rust async last year?", "find Show HN posts about local LLMs" or "summarize the comments on this HN thread" from the HN archive, instead of guessing.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own when the user asks what Hacker News has said or posted about a topic, person or product over time, or what people said in a particular HN thread, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter. What's trending on HN right now goes to [`hottest-tech-discussions`](#claude-code-agent-hottest-tech-discussions).
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "hn-search"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Reduces the question to a few distinctive keywords (in the form people write them, since words match whole), and picks `--type` (stories for threads, comments for opinions, Ask/Show HN when asked), `--sort` (relevance for the big threads, date for "lately"), and any date window, minimum score or author from the user's wording.
2. Runs `python3 hn_search.py "QUERY" [...] --json` from the repo root — for "what does HN think of X?", a story search to find the biggest threads and a comment search for recent views.
   - Keeps comment searches on topic, because a word matches in any sense. It prefers searching inside the topic's own threads (`--story`), and gives an ambiguous name one context word that only fits the meaning wanted (`"swift xcode"`, `"bun node"`, `"golang"`), checking that the word hasn't brought in a meaning of its own (`"swift apple"` finds Taylor Swift). Because a context word also biases the sample towards its own topic (`"swift xcode"` finds Xcode complaints), it runs two such searches with different words for opinions, gives more weight to views found in both, and never reports a context word's topic as a finding. It relies on the script's default of leaving out the monthly job threads, passing `--include-job-threads` only when the user asks about jobs, and skips comments that use the word in another sense or matched on the author's name, and fetches extra results to make up for them.
3. Reads a thread the user linked, or the biggest one or two a search found, with `python3 hn_thread.py ITEM [...] --json`; for "what did that thread say about X?", searches all of the thread's comments with `hn_search.py "X" --story ITEM` instead: the top comments in HN's ranked order with a few replies each, more for a very large thread.
4. Reports threads as a compact list (title, score, comments, date, links), or summarizes the views in the comments it retrieved — for a thread, saying how many of its highest-ranked comments it read — grouping similar opinions, noting disagreement, linking its sources and saying how many comments it read and how many it skipped as off-topic — a sample, not a consensus. An exit code 2 means it fixes the arguments rather than retrying; a network-looking failure is retried at most once; an empty result is reported as such, with a broader search suggested or run.

### Configuration

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying scripts | [`hn_search.py`](#hn_searchpy), [`hn_thread.py`](#hn_threadpy) |

### Notes / limitations

- Requires `hn_search.py`, `hn_thread.py`, their `hn.py` / `cli_utils.py` dependencies, and the `requests` package to be runnable from the repo root.
- Inherits the limitations of [`hn_search.py`](#hn_searchpy) (whole-word matching, text-first relevance ranking, no comment scores, 100 results per search) and [`hn_thread.py`](#hn_threadpy) (a big thread is read as a sample of its highest-ranked comments).
- A summary of "what HN thinks" is only as representative as the handful of comments retrieved.
- Being a prompt rather than code, the agent isn't covered by the offline test suite. [`docs/features/agent/hn-search/test-runs.md`](docs/features/agent/hn-search/test-runs.md) lists targeted live runs of its judgement rules (a distinctive and an ambiguous name, hiring demand, a linked thread, "lately", no matches, a topic inside one thread); all passed on 2026-10-21. They found that comment searches by relevance return an arbitrary slice (comments have no score) and that hiring demand is best measured inside each monthly thread; the agent's instructions now cover both.
- Its off-topic filtering is the agent's own judgment, not a feature of the script, so an occasional off-topic comment can still get through, especially for names with many meanings.

---

## stock_tech_buzz_agent.py

An agent script that combines [`market_top_volume.py`](#market_top_volumepy) and [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy): it pulls the top 10 highest-volume stocks on **both** NYSE and Nasdaq (20 stocks total), pulls the 50 hottest tech discussions from Hacker News (`--discussion-limit`, default 50; the underlying script ranks 4× that many top stories), and reports which of those stocks are actually being talked about — pairing each matched stock with the discussion(s) that mention it. No API key or authentication required.

Requires `market_top_volume.py` and `hottest_tech_discussions.py` to be present in the same directory — it imports them directly rather than shelling out.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/)
- the repo's own `cli_utils.py` module (no install — run from the repo root so it imports)

```bash
pip install requests
```

### Usage

```bash
python3 stock_tech_buzz_agent.py [--stock-limit N] [--discussion-limit N] [--json]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `--stock-limit N` | 10 | Number of top-volume stocks to pull *per exchange* (so total candidates = 2×N); must be 1 or more |
| `--discussion-limit N` | 50 | Number of hottest tech discussions to search. `hottest_tech_discussions.py` inspects 4× this many top stories (200 for the default 50), so larger values take longer; must be 1 or more |
| `--json` | off | Print machine-readable JSON to stdout instead of a human-readable report — for calling this script as a tool from an agent or another program |

#### Example output

```
Fetching top 10 NYSE + top 10 Nasdaq stocks by volume, and searching the top 50 tech discussions...

1. NVDA - NVIDIA Corporation (NASDAQ)
   Volume: 92,250,395  |  Price: 181.24  |  Change: +1.87%
   - "NVIDIA unveils new AI chip" (Score: 512, Comments: 231)
     https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=12345678

...
```

If no top-volume stock is mentioned in any of the fetched discussions (the common case — ticker mentions in HN titles are relatively rare), it prints:

```
No overlap found between the top 20 volume stocks and the top 39 tech discussions.
```

(The discussion count in that message can be slightly lower than `--discussion-limit`: the underlying [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy) ranks 4× that many top stories, but stories whose detail fetch fails, and non-story items such as jobs, drop out.)

#### Tool usage (`--json`)

```bash
python3 stock_tech_buzz_agent.py --json
```

```json
[
  {
    "symbol": "NVDA",
    "name": "NVIDIA Corporation",
    "exchange": "NASDAQ",
    "volume": 92250395,
    "price": 216.85,
    "change_percent": -0.32635,
    "discussions": [
      {
        "title": "NVIDIA unveils new AI chip",
        "score": 512,
        "comments": 234,
        "discussion_url": "https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=12345678"
      }
    ]
  }
]
```

With `--json`, the leading progress line is suppressed and results print as a JSON array (empty `[]` if no stock was mentioned) on stdout. On failure, an exit code of `1` is returned and a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) is printed to stderr instead of plain text.

### How it works

1. **Fetch top-volume stocks** — Calls `market_top_volume.get_movers()` with metric `"volume"` once for `"nyse"` and once for `"nasdaq"` (each with `--stock-limit`), tagging each quote with its source exchange and de-duplicating by symbol.
2. **Fetch tech discussions** — Calls `hottest_tech_discussions.get_hottest_tech_discussions()` with `--discussion-limit`. That function inspects the first `4 × --discussion-limit` of HN's current top stories (200 for the default 50) before ranking by score.
3. **Match stocks to discussions** — For every stock, checks every discussion's title for either: (a) the ticker symbol as a case-sensitive whole word (e.g. `NVDA`), or (b) the company name — with legal-entity suffixes like "Corporation"/"Inc."/"Ltd." stripped — as a case-insensitive whole-word/phrase match (e.g. "NVIDIA Corporation" → "NVIDIA").
4. **Filter and report** — Keeps only stocks with at least one matching discussion, and prints each stock paired with the discussion(s) that mentioned it.

### Configuration

These are set as constants near the top of the file — edit them directly to change behavior:

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `STOCK_LIMIT_PER_EXCHANGE` | 10 | Stocks pulled per exchange before matching |
| `DISCUSSION_LIMIT` | 50 | Discussions searched for mentions |
| `EXCHANGES` | `("nyse", "nasdaq")` | Exchanges queried via `market_top_volume.get_movers()` |
| `AMBIGUOUS_SYMBOLS` | e.g. `{"AI", "ON", "IT", "ALL", ...}` | Ticker symbols excluded from symbol-matching because they double as common English words/acronyms (company-name matching still applies to these) |
| `NAME_SUFFIXES` | regex of legal-entity suffixes | Suffixes stripped from company names before matching (e.g. "Corporation", "Inc.", "Ltd.") |

### API reference

- `get_top_volume_stocks(limit_per_exchange=10)` — Fetches and combines top-volume quotes from both exchanges, de-duplicated by symbol.
- `normalize_company_name(name)` — Strips a trailing legal-entity suffix from a company name.
- `stock_mentions_in_title(quote, title)` — Returns `True` if a quote's ticker symbol or normalized company name appears in a discussion title.
- `find_stock_buzz(limit_per_exchange=10, discussion_limit=50)` — Orchestrates fetching both data sources and matching; returns `(results, stocks, discussions)`.
- `result_to_dict(result)` — Flattens one `{stock, discussions}` match into the record used for **both** output modes (nested discussion URLs come from [`hn.py`](#hnpy)'s `discussion_url`, reached through `hottest_tech_discussions`).
- `format_result(rank, row)` — Renders a `result_to_dict()` record as a text block: symbol, name and exchange; volume, price and % change; then each matching discussion's title, score, comment count and link.
- `parse_args(argv=None)` — Parses `--stock-limit`, `--discussion-limit`, and `--json` CLI flags.
- `main(argv=None)` — Entry point; fetches, handles top-level network errors, and prints results.

### Error handling

- If fetching either the stock data or the discussion data fails (network error, timeout, non-2xx response, or a malformed screener payload), the script prints an error and exits with status code 1 — plain text on stderr normally, or a JSON object (`{"error": "..."}`) on stderr when `--json` is passed.
- A `--stock-limit` or `--discussion-limit` that isn't a positive integer is rejected by argparse (exit code 2) before any network call.
- If no stock is mentioned in any discussion, the script prints a "No overlap found..." message (or `[]` with `--json`) and exits normally — this is expected and common, not an error condition.

### Notes / limitations

- Matching is title-only — it does not inspect discussion body text, comments, or the linked article itself, so mentions buried in the article or comment thread are missed.
- Ticker-symbol matching is case-sensitive and whole-word to cut down on false positives, but is inherently heuristic: an all-caps acronym coincidentally matching a real ticker (outside the curated `AMBIGUOUS_SYMBOLS` list) could still produce a false positive, and a legitimate mention using unusual casing could be missed.
- Company-name matching only strips one legal-entity suffix; a distinctive-enough remaining name (e.g. "NVIDIA", "Moderna") is a solid signal, but this hasn't been tuned against every possible company name shape.
- Inherits the limitations of both underlying scripts — see [`market_top_volume.py`](#market_top_volumepy) and [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy)'s own Notes / limitations sections.

---

## app/hottest_discussions/

A small local web app at `app/hottest_discussions/hottest_discussions_server.py`, separate from the CLI scripts above. It serves a page that starts **empty**, with a "Show" drop-down (10 / 25 / 50, default 10) and a **Refresh** button. Refresh calls the server's `/api/discussions` endpoint, which runs [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy) directly (`get_hottest_tech_discussions()` + `story_to_dict()`) and returns the same rows as that script's `--json`, so the page and the CLI always rank the same way. See [`app/hottest_discussions/README.md`](app/hottest_discussions/README.md) for the full walkthrough and [`app/hottest_discussions/docs/features.md`](app/hottest_discussions/docs/features.md) for the feature spec.

### Requirements

- Python 3.7+
- [`requests`](https://pypi.org/project/requests/) (used by `hottest_tech_discussions.py`)

### Usage

```bash
python3 app/hottest_discussions/hottest_discussions_server.py [--host HOST] [--port PORT]
```

| Flag | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `--host HOST` | `127.0.0.1` | Interface to bind |
| `--port PORT` | `8001` | Port to listen on (8001 so it can run alongside `app/stock_information/` on 8000) |

#### Example

```bash
python3 app/hottest_discussions/hottest_discussions_server.py
# Serving Hottest Tech Discussions at http://127.0.0.1:8001/ (Ctrl+C to stop)
```

Open <http://127.0.0.1:8001/>, pick a count from **Show**, and click **Refresh**. The page calls `GET /api/discussions?limit=N`, which returns JSON: `{limit, fetched_at, discussions: [{rank, title, score, comments, posted, url, discussion_url}, ...]}`.

### How it works

1. `GET /` serves the inline HTML page with no discussions.
2. On Refresh, the page calls `GET /api/discussions?limit=N`.
3. The handler checks `limit` is 10, 25 or 50 (missing means 10), then calls `hottest_tech_discussions.get_hottest_tech_discussions(limit)` and flattens each story with `story_to_dict()`.
4. The page renders the rows as cards (rank, linked title, points, comments, posted time, HN discussion link) and shows the fetch time in the footer.

### Configuration

| Constant | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `LIMIT_OPTIONS` | `(10, 25, 50)` | The drop-down's and the API's allowed counts; the first entry is `RESULTS_TO_SHOW`, imported from `hottest_tech_discussions.py` |
| `DEFAULT_PORT` | `8001` | Port when `--port` isn't passed |

### API reference

- `render_limit_options(selected=10)` / `render_page()` — Build the drop-down options and the page.
- `parse_limit(value)` — Returns `value` as an int from `LIMIT_OPTIONS`, else raises `ValueError`.
- `get_discussions(limit)` — Runs the script and returns `{limit, fetched_at, discussions}`.
- `HottestDiscussionsHandler` — Serves `/` and `/api/discussions`.
- `parse_args(argv=None)` / `main(argv=None)` — `--host`/`--port` parsing and the serve loop.

### Error handling

- `limit` other than 10/25/50 → HTTP 400; Hacker News unreachable or a malformed response → HTTP 502. Either way the page shows `Refresh failed: <message>` and keeps the current list.
- Bad `--port` is rejected by argparse (exit code 2).

### Notes / limitations

- Inherits the same "hottest" definition as [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy): highest score among the first 4× N of HN's current top stories, no tech-specific keyword filtering, live-snapshot results that differ between clicks.
- Nothing appears until Refresh is clicked — no polling or auto-refresh.
- Needs the server running (it replaced the earlier static `generate_page.py` / `index.html`, whose JavaScript copy of the ranking had drifted from the script). Choosing 50 fetches 200 stories' details, so it's slower than 10.
- Binds to localhost by default; `--host 0.0.0.0` exposes it with no authentication.

---

## Claude Code agent: `hottest-tech-discussion-summary`

A [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code) subagent definition at `.claude/agents/hottest-tech-discussion-summary.md`, built against the feature spec in `docs/features/agent/hottest-tech-discussion-summary/features.md`. Unlike the other agents in this repo, it isn't a thin wrapper around one script — it chains four tools ([`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy), [`hn_thread.py`](#hn_threadpy), two of the six `web_search_*.py` engines, and [`url_content.py`](#url_contentpy)) with its own reasoning between each call, to turn a plain list of trending discussions into researched, sourced summaries.

### Purpose

Lets Claude Code answer requests like "summarize today's hottest tech discussions", "research the top 3 trending tech topics and summarize each with sources" or "…and how HN discussed them before" — going well beyond the headline list the plain [`hottest-tech-discussions`](#claude-code-agent-hottest-tech-discussions) agent reports, by researching each topic and citing what it found.

### How it's invoked

- **Automatically** — Claude Code selects this subagent on its own when the user wants each top topic researched and summarized with sources, rather than just a list of what's trending, based on the `description` field in its frontmatter.
- **Explicitly** — via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type: "hottest-tech-discussion-summary"`.

Subagent definitions are loaded when a Claude Code session starts, so a newly added or edited agent file only takes effect in sessions started afterward — not the session it was created in.

### What it does

1. Runs `python3 hottest_tech_discussions.py --json --limit N` to get the top N hottest discussions, where N is however many topics the requester asked for, defaulting to 3 if they didn't say.
2. For each of the N, reads the discussion and writes a summary of it from whatever it retrieved:
   - **The HN thread** with `python3 hn_thread.py ITEM --top 10 --replies 1 --max-chars 400 --json`, for its highest-ranked comments. HN refuses `url_content.py`'s requests for `news.ycombinator.com/item?id=…` pages with HTTP 419; `hn_thread.py` goes through the Algolia API instead.
   - **The linked article**, when there is one, with `python3 url_content.py "URL" --text --max-chars 15000 --json`.
   - A page that returns status 200 but almost no content (just a site name, or a JavaScript / sign-in wall, as Reddit pages usually do) counts as not retrieved. So does a page that is mostly a sign-in wall with a few headlines or teaser lines around it, as LinkedIn profiles return.
3. Judges (itself — no script does this) a short, search-friendly phrase capturing the discussion's actual subject from its title.
4. Searches that phrase with two of the six `web_search_*.py` scripts, tried in the same priority order the [`web-search`](#claude-code-agent-web-search) agent uses (Brave → Serper → Tavily → SerpApi → Exa → DuckDuckGo), taking up to 3 distinct-URL results from the first engine that succeeds and up to 3 more from the next engine that succeeds — up to 6 results total from 2 separate sources, not one merged pool of 3. It asks each engine for 8 so it can skip results that add nothing: the topic's own article or HN thread, which search engines often return; sites that never give `url_content.py` any content (Reddit, Instagram, Facebook, YouTube and TikTok came back with 4–195 characters in testing, while X posts do return their text); and results clearly about a different subject (earlier releases of the same product count as background, not a different subject). The report says how many it skipped and why (never `web_search_perplexity.py`, which doesn't fit this flow's flat result shape).
5. Retrieves each search result with `python3 url_content.py "URL" --text --max-chars 6000 --json`, using `--text` to get readable content rather than raw HTML. Near-empty pages and other HN pages count as not retrieved, as in step 2. A result whose `final_url` matches a page already collected (two search URLs can redirect to the same page) is a duplicate, replaced by the next spare result. A result that only links elsewhere (a repost of the headline) doesn't count as content. A failed, near-empty or pointer result is replaced once by the engine's next spare too, but stays in the link list marked as dropped. If, after those replacements, none of an engine's results gave real content, it searches that engine once more with a narrower phrase (the subject's own name plus what it is).
6. Writes a second summary per topic — of the subject, from all of the search-result content it successfully retrieved (not just search snippets) — and reports each topic's title and link(s) with the discussion summary, the distilled subject phrase, and the subject summary with its source links, each labelled with the page's own title from `url_content.py --text` (falling back to the search engine's title when the page's is missing or just the site's name, like "Reddit"), noting plainly wherever a step came up short (fewer than N hot discussions, fewer than 3 results from either search engine or only one working engine) instead of filling the gap itself. Every link collected — discussion links and search-result links alike — is preserved in the report even when its content couldn't be fetched, so the requester can check it out later; only the failed link's content is dropped, never the link itself.
7. **Only when the requester asks** how HN discussed the topics before, lists up to 3 earlier HN threads per topic with [`hn_search.py`](#hn_searchpy):
   - It runs `python3 hn_search.py "KEYWORDS" --since <two years ago> --until <three days before the trending thread> --min-points 50 --limit 10 --json`, where `KEYWORDS` is 1–3 distinctive words naming the subject, its own name when it has one (`muse spark`) and the concept otherwise (`mechanistic interpretability`), never the headline. In testing, headline words found nothing useful, and a generic category (`wayland desktop`) found only unrelated threads where the project's former name (`deskwm`) found it.
   - The `--until` gap matters: other submissions of the same news cluster around the day it broke. In testing, every `spyware ads` match came from that same day. The gap also leaves out the trending thread itself.
   - It checks each result is about the same subject and keeps up to the 3 highest-scoring, leaving out threads with almost no discussion (under 20 points or no comments) unless they're the only match. It doesn't pad the list to three. If nothing relevant comes back, or only threads about the general field, it tries one more specific search: the project's or a former name, the company or author, or the idea at stake. Then it reports them, or says none were found.
   - It's search only; it doesn't read or summarize the earlier threads.
8. Leads the report with a timestamp (current date) so a given run's output is clearly distinguishable from a summary produced on a different day.
9. Ends by offering the user a choice — publish the summary as an artifact, save it as a local HTML file or a markdown file, or do nothing further — without acting on any of them itself; the calling Claude Code session (which has the tools this research-only agent doesn't) carries out whichever the user picks.

### Configuration

The agent's frontmatter restricts it to the `Bash` tool only, since running scripts and reading their stdout is all it needs.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `tools` | `Bash` |
| Underlying scripts | [`hottest_tech_discussions.py`](#hottest_tech_discussionspy), [`hn_thread.py`](#hn_threadpy), two of the six `web_search_*.py` engines, [`url_content.py`](#url_contentpy); [`hn_search.py`](#hn_searchpy) only when earlier threads are asked for |

### Notes / limitations

- Every `url_content.py` call passes `--respect-robots`, because the agent picks the article and search-result URLs itself. A page whose robots.txt disallows the fetch or opts out of AI input (`Content-Signal: ai-input=no`) is never fetched: its link is kept and labelled "blocked by the site's robots.txt", it is never summarized from its search snippet, and a blocked search result is replaced once from the engine's spares like any other dropped result.
- Requires all of `hottest_tech_discussions.py`, `hn_thread.py`, the `web_search_*.py` scripts, and `url_content.py` (plus `beautifulsoup4`, for `url_content.py --text`) to be present and runnable from the repo root, and at least two working search-engine API keys to satisfy the "3 results each from 2 sources" requirement.
- The subject-phrase distillation is a judgment call made by the agent itself, not a deterministic script — the same discussion could be phrased as slightly different search queries across runs.
- Inherits the limitations of each underlying tool: `hottest_tech_discussions.py`'s live-snapshot ranking (results differ between runs), the `web_search_*.py` fallback chain's per-engine quirks (documented in the [`web-search`](#claude-code-agent-web-search) agent section), and `url_content.py --text`'s blunt extraction (no paragraph breaks, boilerplate not removed) plus its usual single-attempt-GET/no-retry behavior.
- A slow-running workflow by construction — up to N discussions × (1 discussion-list call + 1 `hn_thread.py` read, a few requests of its own, + up to 1 article fetch + up to 2 search calls + up to 6 search-result content fetches) — since it's several sequential network round-trips with reasoning in between, not a single script invocation. Asking for earlier HN threads adds 1–2 `hn_search.py` calls per topic (each of which can itself take a few requests to leave out job threads).
- Being a prompt rather than code, the agent isn't covered by the offline test suite. Instead, [`docs/features/agent/hottest-tech-discussion-summary/test-runs.md`](docs/features/agent/hottest-tech-discussion-summary/test-runs.md) lists targeted live runs: each gives the agent one fixed topic chosen to trigger one of its edge-case rules (the rephrased search, replacing dropped results from the spares, the sign-in-wall check), with what a correct run looks like and when it last passed.
- The earlier-threads keywords are, like the subject phrase, the agent's own judgment. A topic with no distinctive name, or one that's genuinely new, often finds nothing, and the report says so.
- The closing "publish as an artifact / save as a local file / do nothing" offer is only ever a prompt from this agent — since its frontmatter restricts it to `Bash`, it has no `Artifact` or `Write` tool access itself, so the calling Claude Code session must be the one to act once the user picks an option.
